Added 2026-09-02, out of the repaired Blender research.** Cycles' own list of cards comes back **empty** in all four versions on a factory-fresh Blender — with an untouched settings folder, and again started at factory settings — and fills only once a refresh has been asked for, the preferences panel has been drawn, or a preferences file saved earlier is read. **So a machine asked before anything else says it can see no cards, and a four-card machine and a cardless one give the same answer.** Label: **observed in a test** — `Preparation/Blender Research/Across topics/Running Blender with nobody there.md`, *The machine 5* (see [Sources](Sources/Server%20Setup.sources.md)).

**Nothing this document reads is that list.** [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 1 takes card presence from the PCI vendor scan and the driver's own query (Fact 46; `Servers` H11's table), item 5's driver check is `nvidia-smi`'s exit, and [W4](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine) reads a finished job's recorded device line rather than any list — none of them is a Blender reading, so no step, statement or ending below moves. **The rule, stated because the empty answer carries no mark distinguishing it from a real one:** where anything reads Blender's own device list, **the refresh is asked for first**, and **an unrefreshed empty list is never read as *this machine has no cards*** — the only thing it establishes is that nothing filled it. `Servers` H16 declines the route outright and this is a fourth reason for that decline. **The conflict the finding sits against is [Section 13](#13-unresolved) item 25, and it is not resolved.**

**R1b. The driver follows every Blender on the server, however and whenever it got there, and the swap waits until nothing on the server is rendering or waiting to render. Added 2026-10-11, later the same day, the Creator's ruling (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's rulings on OptiX and the driver, 2026-10-11.md`, its last section): *"Yes, that's a good call. Don't build anything yet, just put everything in the plans."* Label: Creator-stated.** The swap is [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 5's and is unchanged: the same machines (the systemd kind, on Ubuntu), the same route, the same check-then-restart, the same ending where it misses the bar. This clause says when it is made.

- **The comparison** is the driver's version (`Servers` H11b's reading) against `Rendering` L23a's bar for the newest Blender on the server. It is taken where the Blenders are already being read, and nowhere else:
  - in every ready action, at item 5, including one that installs no Blender (item 5, *The bar*);
  - when an install from the Blender section reaches *done*, by either route — the list ([B4](#51-the-list-and-the-fetch)) or the user's own build ([B7](#52-a-build-the-user-points-at)) — with the version the published build answered; and the same when a ready action published a build whose version was known only at its answer;
  - at each of [B12](#54-the-search)'s moments for the search — Server Setup's view opened, `Render Settings` asking for the version list, before a removal — the same reading [B12b](#54-the-search)'s note is drawn from.

  **Never at connection.** [B12](#54-the-search) keeps the search off the connection, and this clause adds no search of its own. A Blender placed on the server some other way — by hand, or by the distribution's own software — is compared at the next of these moments.
- **What the comparison concludes is held for the connected period and never stored** (`State, identity and recovery` D4, as [B12](#54-the-search) is). *A swap is owed* where the driver answers below the bar on a machine item 5 would swap on. A later comparison replaces it.
- **The live set decides when.** It is `Queue & History` §17's: the job records with state *waiting*, *held* or *rendering*. **A transfer under way between this desktop and the machine counts too**, because a restart interrupts it ([T7](#8-the-restart)) — the same reading the Whole app spec gives *running* for the application's own update (Row 518; `Desktop app and delivery` Z31). *That half is this plan pass's own decision, not the Creator's words.* Where nothing is live, the swap is made at once. Where anything is, it waits. **A job held for the swap is left out of this set** ([R1c](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of)): it is the job the swap is for, and counting it would hold the swap back for ever. *Added 2026-10-11, later still, the Creator's ruling that a job needing the newer driver is held back; this read the three states with nothing left out.* The same set is read at the last guard below, on the same terms.
- **Who starts a waiting swap, and when: the desktop, and only while connected.** It starts at the reading that shows the last of that machine's live work ended — `State, identity and recovery` F15's third moment, the queue reading a connected period already takes, where `Desktop app and delivery` V12 retries a waiting update — or at the end of the last transfer (F15's first moment), whichever comes later. Nothing polls, nothing is timed, and nothing reaches out. A connected period that ends with a swap owed loses it, since it is period-held, and the next comparison above finds it again.
- **Outside a ready action the swap is a run of its own**: a machine-changing setup run ([Y1](#31-two-classes-of-setup-run)) of the readings, item 5's swap, item 6's restart and *done*, under [Section 3](#3-the-setup-run-its-worker-and-the-recorded-safe-boundaries)'s and [Section 4](#4-interruption-resumption-and-the-safe-re-run)'s discipline and [Y9](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)'s one run at a time. An install run stays additive, as Y1 has it; the swap follows it as a second run rather than joining it, so [Y4](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries)'s boundary order holds. Its readings read the driver's version again, and where it now meets the bar nothing is done.
- **Elevation comes before the state.** The run probes ([M3](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each)) before the machine-level state is set, and where a password is owed and none is held, [E2](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password)'s ask is made then. A declined ask makes no swap, and the swap is not asked for again in that connected period.
- **The last guard comes after the state is set.** Once the machine-level state stands, which holds every admission (`Rented-server runtime` P2, A11), the live set is read again before the swap's first act. Where it is no longer empty, no swap is made: a ready action carries on without it, and a swap run ends at *done* with nothing done. Either way the swap waits again. This closes the gap between the reading and the act, as G21 does for the update (`Desktop app and delivery` V11).
- **What the user sees while it waits is [B12b](#54-the-search)'s note, and nothing else.** The note is true until the swap is made. No statement is raised for a waiting swap: `Servers` H11b speaks only where Smoozy cannot change the driver, and here it can. **The restart is told as every setup restart is**: the server reads *restarting* ([T4](#8-the-restart), [T6](#8-the-restart)). *And where a job is held for the swap, that job's entry says so ([R1c](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of); `Queue & History` H12). Added 2026-10-11, later still, the Creator's ruling that the user is told on the job in the queue.*
- **Nothing else moves.** A swap that misses the bar, whichever run made it, ends as item 5 says, and the user is told in H11b's words. *Added 2026-10-11, the runner's ruling (`Audits/Architecture/Server Setup/Decisions record.md`, its last entry), written by the builder of piece O4: **a missed swap is not retried on its own** — the miss stands for that server until the user's next ready action on it, and until then no comparison above concludes that a swap is owed there.* On a container nothing is swapped and H11b speaks. Debian has no swap ([Section 13](#13-unresolved) item 28). The swap never warns under [W3](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine), because it never runs over live work.
- **How it was built, where the clause left the choice. Added 2026-10-12 at Build, the piece that builds this clause; no auditor has seen this text.** The swap's own run is **a run of the ready action's own kind** ([Y6b](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries)), composed of the readings, item 5, item 6 and *done*, so a machine program of any release takes it and no new kind reaches the machine. **It takes no stopped setup over**: where a setup the user left part-way stands, the machine refuses the fresh run and nothing is done; carrying that setup on stays the user's *Set up again* ([S3](#41-what-a-part-finished-setup-reports), [S4](#42-the-recovery-run)). **The comparison outside a ready action concludes *owed* on the systemd kind** (`Rented-server runtime` K1's own-kernel reading), because the family is not held between runs; on Debian the swap run's own readings then make no swap and end with the bar missed, which is what tells the user in H11b's words. **The comparison after an install is taken by the search**, held for the Set up tab so the table drawn next does not take it again, so its bar is still the newest Blender's. **The automatic downloads count as a transfer under way from the moment a file is owed until the last owed file has come down**, across the pauses between their passes, so the swap never lands between two passes of an ending's downloads. **A declined ask at a ready action's driver step also stops the swap being asked for again in that connected period.** **A queue reading that could not be had is read as live**, as `Desktop app and delivery` V11 reads a work reading it could not have.
- **The swap's run is not begun on a machine it cannot act on. Added 2026-11-03 at Build, out of the Sesterce live pass; no auditor has seen this text.** A stopped setup with the state standing, a comparison still holding the connection's first driver reading, and the refusal above made a loop: each attempt took the exclusion, so the card read *Setting up* with *Cancel setup*; the refusal drew *A setup stopped part-way* with no control; the ending let the held search go, and the next search or queue reading tried again, for seven minutes (**observed**). **So before the exclusion is taken, the desktop reads the machine**: `Rented-server runtime` V9's health answer, and the driver's own query. **Where a setup stands, or the driver gives no version, no run is begun, nothing is told, and what the comparison concluded is forgotten for the period**; the next comparison finds it again if it is still true. The ready action's own item 5 is what carries the driver on a *Set up again*. **A refusal that still arrives**, a state set between that reading and the opening, is treated the same way: forgotten, and the card reads the machine again rather than saying a second ending. **A stopped run this copy drove that left no state counts the same**: it stands to be carried on by *Set up again* ([S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 5), and a swap's run opened over it would replace it. **So the card a live period draws after a failed step is the card a reopen draws**, because nothing else begins on the server.

**R1c. A job that would meet the old driver is held from submission until the swap's run ends, and the desktop releases it then. Added 2026-10-11, later still, the Creator's ruling (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's rulings on OptiX and the driver, 2026-10-11.md`, its last section): *"Yes, good idea. But also the user has to be notified about it somehow, probably the job in the queue should get a warning of some kind or something."* Label: Creator-stated.** It closes the gap [R1b](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) left: a job sent for that Blender while the server was busy met the old driver and failed, because the swap waited for it. The hold is `Rented-server runtime` §8's one mechanism, origin *submission*, as the first-frame check's second job has it; no second waiting mechanism exists (`Job files and outputs` H3).

- **What is held, decided on the desktop at submission and nowhere else.** A scene-job is held where all four stand as its submission is composed: its launch would name `OPTIX` (`Rendering` L21, L23 — a ~~cards job~~ Cycles job on the cards on an NVIDIA machine, corrected 2026-11-02), read from the resolved device choice the submission carries (`Render Settings` D1, as `Queue & History` E12a holds it), so a job whose device is not known at submission is not held, *and only a Cycles job: this clause's bullet of 2026-11-02*; the driver's version is below `Rendering` L23a's bar for the job's own Blender, the version its pick answered — the comparison [B12b](#54-the-search)'s note on that row is drawn from; R1b's comparison concludes *a swap is owed* for this connected period; and `Servers` H11b's statement does not stand for this server. **So nothing is held on a container, on Debian, or on a server whose swap missed the bar**: none of them owes a swap, or H11b already says Smoozy cannot change the driver there, and a job sent goes as `Rendering` §9 item 3 has it. Where the desktop holds no comparison for the period, nothing is held either. **The machine decides nothing**: the supervisor places the hold the submission names. The composition is `Rendering` S18a.
- **A held job is not live work for R1b**, and only for R1b. It stays live for every other reader of `Queue & History` §17 — `File Browser`'s warnings and [B14](#55-taking-a-blender-off)'s removal warning among them.
- **A standing hold of this reason is a moment for R1b's comparison.** The queue reading (`Queue & History` A1) that finds one, on any connection, compares the driver against the bar for the held job's own Blender, the version its record carries (`Queue & History` N19). That needs no search, so [B12](#54-the-search)'s rule that the search stays off the connection is untouched. Where a swap is owed, R1b's starting rule applies. **This is what lets a hold placed in one connected period be released in a later one.**
- **Release comes at the ending of the run whose item 5 compared and acted** — a ready action or a swap run on that server, where the swap was attempted or the readings found the driver already at the bar. The desktop then releases every hold of this reason on that server by U9's ordinary release command, the answer *go on*, one at a time in the order the jobs were submitted, each sent once the one before has a recorded outcome the desktop has read, as `Rendering` S4a serializes submissions. So the jobs keep their order among themselves as each joins the line (`Queue & History` H3). The desktop learns the run's ending live, or, where it was not connected, at the first reading of the last setup on the next connection ([Y6](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries)). **A run that backed off at the last guard, a declined elevation, and a run cut off part-way release nothing**: the swap waits again, and so do the holds.
- **Where the run ended without the driver at the bar** — the swap missed it, the run failed, or the driver no longer answers — **the holds are released all the same.** Each job then meets what `Rendering` §9 item 3 says of a job below its bar. Nothing else is tried, and the fall-back to `CUDA` stays parked by the Creator. Before sending each release the desktop writes `Queue & History` N23's mark on that job's local record, so the failure that follows is not left unexplained.
- **What the user sees** is the held job's entry (`Queue & History` H12), beside [B12b](#54-the-search)'s note on the Blender's row. A phone is sent no message about this hold, and its status reads as the entry does (`Telegram` §17 A3); the job's ending is told like any other. **Cancelling the job, and everything else the user can do to a held job, is `Queue & History` H12's.**
- **Nothing else moves.** The swap, its moments, its last guard and its endings are R1 item 5's and R1b's. A container, Debian, and a server where H11b's statement stands are unchanged: no holding, and the job fails as `Rendering` §9 item 3 says, on a server already marked.
- **How it was built, where the clause left the choice. Added 2026-10-12 at Build, held-job piece A; no auditor has seen this text.** **A fifth condition at submission: the server's program is this application's own release** (none of `Desktop app and delivery` V13's conditions stands). A program from before this piece refuses a hold with no dependency by name, which would refuse the whole submission, so where the program is older the job goes plain and meets `Rendering` §9 item 3 as before. **Item 5 *compared and acted*** is read as: the driver step ran on the systemd kind with a bar to read against, past both readings of the live set, so the swap was attempted or the readings found the driver at the bar. A run that failed after that point releases with N23's mark, unless its last check had already read the driver at the bar; a run that failed before it releases nothing. **The run's ending learned on a later connection** is read from what that run left rather than from the setup record, which does not keep whether item 5 acted: at the queue reading of any connection — the connection's own first reading and the live pass — a standing hold whose job's bar the driver now meets is released with no mark, and one whose bar it is still below where H11b's statement stands is released with the mark. **The release sequence stops at the first job not released** — its mark refused, or its outcome not read — so no later job joins the line ahead of it; the rest wait for the next moment. **N23's write needs the job's local record**, so a held job this copy holds no record of is never released by it, and its user cancels it. **§13 item 29's question is settled: the swap run needs no newer program** (R1b's dated bullet: it is the ready action's own kind, and it places no package), so the hold cannot keep the swap from running; it still holds V11's update until the release.
- **Only a Cycles job is held. Added 2026-11-02 at Build, out of the first 5.3 live pass, the build runner's fix; no auditor of this document has seen it.** ~~*Its launch would name `OPTIX`* is too wide alone, because `Rendering` L21 reads the device and not the engine: an EEVEE job whose device resolves to the cards carries `--cycles-device OPTIX` on its line too.~~ **Corrected later the same day, the build runner's ruling: `Rendering` L21 now names the technology for a Cycles job alone, so the first bullet's *its launch would name `OPTIX`* says this as written.** The bar is Cycles' (`Rendering` L23a), so an EEVEE job was held for a swap it does not need. **So at submission the desktop asks: is the engine the job will use Cycles** — the user's pick on the payload (`Render Settings` E1), else the file's own for the job's scene as the reading in force gives it (`Render Settings` E4), which is what the Render Settings page and the render's start both resolve. EEVEE, or a third engine the file names, is not held. **Where the engine is not known at submission** — no pick, and no reading of the job's scene — **the device alone decides, as it did before this bullet**: the user's own *GPU* on an unread file is held, and a file's own device unread is not. The desktop cannot tell there what the launch will name; the machine can, at the attempt, from its own reading (`Rendering` L21). So a job held on this rule that turns out not to be Cycles waits for a swap it did not need, and then renders with nothing named. ~~**Not established:** that an EEVEE job renders with `OPTIX` on its line below the bar. On 575.64.03 and 5.3.0 Beta `fd2b7d57ef87`, a Cycles job with that line got past start-up and was refused inside its render (*Engine: Cycles*, *Initializing*, then *Found no Cycles device of the specified type*), which points to an EEVEE job never meeting the refusal, and EEVEE did render on the card there. But the record holds no such line for an EEVEE job: that scene's script set no device for it, the page offers none under EEVEE, and its line was not kept. Label: **observed in a test**, for what is said to have been seen (`Audits/Build/Blender 5.3/The live pass — Vast — what happened.md`, item 15). **Breaks:** if Blender refuses that line under EEVEE too, the job ends *Failed*, loudly, rather than waiting for the swap. **What holds:** it is what the same job already does on a server where nothing is held, and the job can be sent again once the driver is replaced.~~ **Struck later the same day: no EEVEE line names `OPTIX` any more, so the question no longer arises.**

**R2. On a bare machine the package placement precedes the ready action**, because the setup-in-progress state is set through the ordinary command path and there is no supervisor to take the command before the package exists. The order on a machine never prepared is: **[M1](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each)'s kind and [M1a](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each)'s family read first and nothing else if either refuses** (added 2026-08-22 — the readings used to sit at the placement, which put them after the hand-over below and made M1a's *nothing is touched* false); then candidate set and hand-over ([R7](#23-what-the-placement-is-given)), the bootstrap-and-repair request placing the package (`Server protocol and transfers` §5; `Rented-server runtime` §2), then the setup run above. **That placement acquires `Rented-server runtime` G1b's one package-transaction claim before inspection or phase change and accepts only G26 to G31's verified manifest entry and exact platform/CPU/libc/ABI tuple. Corrected 2026-08-22 — this used to name G1a, which cannot reach this phase at all.** On a machine never prepared there is **no Smoozy program on the machine to hold a kernel lock**, which is exactly the case G1b was added for: the claim is the desktop's own exclusive create of the same lock file through the file-transfer subsystem, naming the claimant's installation identity. **G1a governs from the moment a verified runtime is alive and performing its own half of the transaction, and not before.** A setup action that meets automatic repair or another application instance already holding the owner reports the in-progress owner and phase and starts no second placement. The placement itself is unprivileged on a systemd machine and carries the elevated hook step on a container (M2).

**R3. On a container the action's shape is verification plus at most the user-space half.** The driver is the host's: never installed, never repaired, never reloaded, and no restart is issued (Fact 48). Where the login can install packages (the container's root), steps 3 and 4 run; where it cannot, nothing elevated runs at all and the action is [W1](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s statements. **The action never reads a CUDA library's own version as evidence the pairing works** — under a deliberate mismatch `cuDriverGetVersion` succeeded and reported CUDA 9.1 while Blender rendered on the processor at exit 0 (Fact 48) — the driver's own answer is the only reading taken.

**R4. What *ready* claims, exactly, and it is cut to what was read.** Where the machine holds a Blender or this run fetched one: that build's library chain resolves (`ldd` clean). **Where there was no Blender to read one against, the library half was not read and *ready* claims nothing about it** — step 4 had no subject ([R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of)) and the action says so rather than composing the claim out of a check that did not run. And, where a card is present, the driver answers. **It does not claim the machine renders on its cards** — with the driver missing or wrong every version rendered on the processor and reported success, so a finished job is no proof and neither is a ready action; only a finished Cycles job asked the right way proves it ([W4](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine); the spec's own bound).

### 2.2 What the action never does

**R5. It never works from Blender's launch error** (one library per round; Fact 47), **never relies on the driver's dependencies** (the metapackage trap), **never restarts unconditionally** (Fact 80), **never reloads modules by hand** (the oscillation), **never trusts the reboot-required flag** (never set on the machine that needed it most), **and never repeats a driver install whose check passes** — the reinstall-becomes-upgrade observation is the exact shape of *running it again makes it worse*, and [S4](#4-interruption-resumption-and-the-safe-re-run) is written so the re-run can never reach it.

### 2.3 What the placement is given

**R6. The candidate set is this document's to supply and `Job files and outputs` 1.1 chooses from it — the root, and, where the set holds more than one candidate, the package area's own location apart from the root (L4a); failing to supply is a fault. Widened 2026-08-22, carrying L4a's hand-over half (scenario replays Pile 1 R12); it used to read *the candidate set for the one root*.** **One set serves both choices and is composed once**: the readings are per path, the second choice draws from what the first leaves, and neither choice is made here. The candidates are the locations established at placement, **taken as they arrive and with no ownership changed to become one**: the mount the connection login's own home area sits on, plus every attached volume. A volume arrives `root:root 755` and refuses the login until an elevated `chown` hands it over ([Two more boxes](Two%20more%20boxes%20—%20the%20volume,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20a%20day%20left%20running.md), Fact 6; `Job files and outputs` L10) — **that hand-over is [R7](#23-what-the-placement-is-given)'s and follows the choice rather than preceding it**, because `Job files and outputs` L2 chooses on a per-path available-space reading taken through `Server protocol and transfers` §9.1's two routes at the candidate's own path, which a `root:root 755` mount permits without owning it. Handing over every attached volume merely to make it a candidate would be an elevated ownership change on the user's machine for mounts that then lose, with nothing recording it and nothing undoing it. **That rule is unchanged by the second choice and is the bound on it**: a candidate that wins neither choice is never handed over, so the widening above adds at most one further owned mount and only where L4a actually placed the package area on it. **Where a candidate's available space cannot be read at all without owning it, the reading is not obtainable, it is said so, and the candidate is dropped from the set** rather than taken over to be measured. The set never includes a memory-backed filesystem, a read-only mount, or a host disk bind-mounted into a container — the 51-filesystem trap (Fact 28).

**R7. The hand-over is performed once, in [R2](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of)'s order, and it is completed before the first job. It covers the mount `Job files and outputs` 1.1 chose for the root, and — only where that document's L4a placed the package area on a second mount — that mount as well.** **The root's own directory is created as a step of this placement, before the hand-over's confirming write and before the five areas — and, where L4a placed the package area on a second mount, that location's own directory the same way. Added 2026-09-04, out of the round-1 code audit of this module's build.** `Job files and outputs` L1 composes the root as the chosen mount plus one segment, its L6 puts four areas directly under it, and its L11 puts the confirming write at the root's exact path — and no clause anywhere told anybody to make that directory, so on a machine never prepared the placement could never finish. **The creation is tried unelevated first**, because on a container whose login cannot install packages nothing elevated runs at all (M2, R3) and such a machine must still be able to place a root under a location it owns; **where the plain create is refused it is made by an elevated constant command through the same planted-link carriage the hand-over already uses ([R7a](#23-what-the-placement-is-given))** — the link's target is the chosen mount, the command names the token and the root's own segment, and **that segment is a build constant of `Job files and outputs` and not a machine-origin value**, so the command string stays fixed at build time and [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one) 1.1 is kept by construction. Each such mount is handed over by its own elevated `chown`, **which names the root's own directory — and, where L4a placed the package area apart, that location's own directory — and never the whole mount. Stated 2026-09-04 with the creation above, and it narrows nothing that was ever performed.** The candidate set R6 supplies includes the mount the connection login's own home area sits on, so a recursive ownership change at the mount would reach files that are not Smoozy's, on the user's own rented machine, with nothing recording it and nothing undoing it — which is the harm R6 already refuses one paragraph earlier for candidates that lose the choice. So that the whole root — and the package area's own location, where L4a put it apart — is then owned by the supervisor's login, **each confirmed by its own real write of at least one byte at its exact path** — never a writability test and never an empty file (L11; `State, identity and recovery` §1.3 rule 4). **The five areas of `Job files and outputs` 1.2 L6 are created so no deep creation is ever needed** (1.3, 2.4 N11): four directly under the root, and the package area at the location L4a gives it, which on a machine offering no second candidate is a fifth sibling under the root like the rest. Creating them is a placement step here. **No mount is ever handed over but the one or two a choice actually landed on.**

**On the ordinary one-mount machine there is no second choice to carry out and this clause is the clause it always was**: one candidate, one root, one `chown`, one confirming write, five areas under that root. **Corrected 2026-08-22, carrying `Job files and outputs` L4a's hand-over half (scenario replays Pile 1 R12); it used to read *there is one hand-over* with *no mount other than the chosen root is ever handed over*, and it said four areas.**

**R7a. The hand-over's carriage, declared against [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one) 1.1's admissibility rule, because a chosen mount's path is a machine-origin path and the step runs before hand-over.** The elevated `chown` is a constant command string and no chosen path ever appears in it. **The owner the command names is this step's other carried value, and its carriage is declared at [E7](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password)** — 1.1's fourth, machine self-derivation, which leaves the string constant and is why this clause speaks of the path alone *(pointer added 2026-09-04)*. The path crosses as the target of a symbolic link at a Smoozy-synthesised token path — 1.1's planted-link carriage: written through the file-transfer subsystem, the link in the login's own writable area and never on the mount it points at, removed when the step ends — and the command names the token. **How `chown` treats a link is cited rather than assumed, because it decides whether the carriage works at all.** Documented — GNU coreutils manual, *chown invocation*, read 2026-08-22 ([Sources](Sources/Server%20Setup.sources.md)): a symbolic link named as an argument is dereferenced by default — *"Do not act on symbolic links themselves but rather on what they point to. This is the default when not operating recursively."* — while under `--recursive` the default reverses, `-P` being *"Do not traverse any symbolic links. This is the default if none of -H, -L, or -P is specified."* — **so the recursive hand-over must carry `-H`**: *"If --recursive (-R) is specified and a command line argument is a symbolic link to a directory, traverse it."* A recursive `chown` through the token without `-H` changes nothing on the mount, and nothing in its exit status says so. **That is why R7's real-write confirmation at the exact target path stays and is the carriage's required landing check**: it is what proves the link resolved where Smoozy planted it and the ownership landed at the target — the command's exit is never that evidence. **The carriage is per mount, so where R7's second hand-over fires the second mount is carried the same way and separately** — its own token path, its own planted link in the login's own writable area and never on the mount it points at, its own removal when the step ends, and its own real-write landing check at the package area's location. Nothing about the second mount is inferred from the first's exit or from the first's landing check. The five areas' creation and the real writes themselves are file-transfer-subsystem operations whose paths are protocol fields and need no command at all. The manual read is the tool project's own and was read on no rental; that a rental's `chown` is coreutils' is the same per-image ground every command-channel step stands on ([Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#12-unresolved) §12 items 3 and 11).

**R7b. The recording is a step of this hand-over, and there is a second recorded path only where there is a second mount. Added 2026-08-22, carrying `Job files and outputs` L4a's hand-over half (scenario replays Pile 1 R12).** The root's path is recorded at this moment, in the two places `Job files and outputs` L1 names and in the form it names them; what is recorded is that document's and is not restated here. **Where L4a placed the package area on a second mount, that location is recorded the same way and in the same durable act as the root's**, so a reading that finds one finds both. **Where L4a's second choice does not fire — one writable candidate, or no remaining candidate clearing the floor L4a applies — the package area is a sibling under the root, its location follows from the root's recorded path, and nothing second is chosen, recorded or handed over.** So this document never composes a second value on a machine that has only one mount to give, and a builder must not read the presence of the root's record as evidence that a second one is owed: which case a machine is in is L4a's, decided once at this hand-over and never re-chosen.

### 2.4 The shadowed user-space half

**R8. On a container, the user-space driver libraries can be shadowed and a broken pairing renders quietly at exit 0** (Fact 48's one dangerous gap). This document's whole defence is R3's reading rule — the driver's own answer, never the library's — plus [W4](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s after-the-job evidence. Nothing here inspects `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` or the symlinks: `ldconfig` silently reverts a swap and almost every package operation fires it, so a reading of that state would not stay true long enough to act on.

### 2.5 Secure Boot, and the machine Smoozy cannot make ready

**R9. Fact 81 is unposable — none of the seven machines this project rented had Secure Boot enforcing — and its failure mode is named as a clause of Row 88's answer rather than a reason to leave the row open.** On a Secure Boot rental the driver step's module signing could fail outright or demand a human enrolling a key at the machine's console, which Smoozy cannot do over the connection it holds. **Such a machine is a machine Smoozy cannot make ready, and the action says so** in [W1](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s could-not-make-ready statement, with the machine's own words behind it. Label: **unresolved**; [Section 13](#13-unresolved) item 1 carries what breaks.

---

## 3. The setup run, its worker, and the recorded safe boundaries

**Constraint:** `Rented-server runtime` X15 — *"For a setup run, termination is at, or recovery from, a recorded safe boundary, and the boundary marker is the per-kind field of that operation record (W4). Its meaning is `Server Setup`'s"* — and §9 entire. The boundaries, their granularity and their meaning are defined here and nowhere else.

### 3.1 Two classes of setup run

**Y1. Every setup act on a machine is a setup run: one setup-kind operation, one operation record written before its worker starts (W3), one recorded execution group.** There are two classes, and which class a run is decides whether the machine-level state is set:

- **A machine-changing run** — and the class is decided **by the act a step performs, never by the step's number**: a run is machine-changing exactly where it performs an elevated package install, a driver act, or a restart. That is what the ready action's [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) items 3 to 6 usually amount to, but the test is the act: a Blender install on a machine lacking the unpack tool performs an elevated package install and is machine-changing, and one on a machine that already has the tool performs an unpack and a rename and is not. A run whose libraries step must install anything is machine-changing by the same test. Such a run sets the machine-level setup-in-progress state naming its own operation identity before its first such step, durably before any restart (P4), and clears it only at *done* (P5). While the state is set, reconciliation does not run and nothing is admitted at machine level (P2, A11); **the one exception, declared here as the state's meaning: the worker start of the run the state names is admitted**, because the state exists to protect that run, not to strangle it. **That exception is narrower than it looks and its scope is stated exactly.** The admission point gates worker starts (A4 — *every worker start passes through it*), and this run's own worker was started before the state was set, so the state never held the run's own steps back and the exception is not needed for them. What it is genuinely needed for is [S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 2's start of the recovery run's worker, which is a worker start made while the state stands. `Rented-server runtime` §9 P2 and §7.2 A11 were written absolute and carried no field for it until 2026-08-22: a seam, recorded at [Section 13](#13-unresolved) item 14 and routed to the runner in [Section 12](#12-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document), and **closed at the Architecture seam close by A11a**, which admits this one worker start and nothing else.
- **An additive run** — a Blender install on a machine that already holds the unpack tool, an add-on install, a Blender removal, Telegram material placement. It sets no machine-level state, **not because none of its steps can disturb any work, but because none of them changes the machine in a way reconciliation or admission must be held back against**: installing a Blender beside a running render left every frame written, installing an add-on changed not one frame value across sixty-four picture-against-picture comparisons — **every one of those installs written into a user repository and none into the folder a running Blender was running from, which is [A6a](#6-add-ons)'s condition and travels with the result** — and deleting the folder a render was running from let the render finish normally (the spec's own observed grounds). A removal does reach live work — a waiting job submitted with that build **will have nothing to render with** — and that is [B14](#55-taking-a-blender-off)'s warning and `Queue & History`'s to act on, not something the state would help with. The observed limit travels with all of it: *these* installs at *this* rate changed no frame, not that installing is harmless in general, and the observation behind the class is an unpack and a rename beside a running render rather than an elevated package install on a busy machine — which is why an install that must reach the package system falls on the other side of the line.

**Y2. Row 110 is kept by the state and its clear, and by nothing this document builds.** The interrupted job is held back because the state is durable before the restart and holds reconciliation and every admission (P4, P2); it is re-run **after** the setup because clearing the state runs reconciliation (P5). The setup and the queue never talk to each other; the state is the only thing between them, held by the machine, so it works with nobody connected. This document's half is [Y1](#31-two-classes-of-setup-run)'s rule about when the state is set and [S6](#4-interruption-resumption-and-the-safe-re-run)'s rule that only a run reaching *done* — or a recovery deliberately abandoning the machine-changing work — clears it.

### 3.2 The recorded safe boundaries

**Y3. A boundary is a named marker in the setup operation record's per-kind field (W4), written durably by the supervisor when the step it names has completed and its verification read has passed, together with the moment on the machine's clock.** Its meaning: **every step up to and including the named one is done and verified; no later step has begun.** Fact 49 shows the machine honouring exactly that meaning — a hard cut names the last boundary passed, never the step it died in.

**Kept at the machine from 2026-09-22, at Build.** The meaning is a rule the supervisor enforces rather than one it trusts the desktop for: **no boundary is written, and no worker step is run, while a boundary composed before it has not been reached** — *done* least of all. [S6](#4-interruption-resumption-and-the-safe-re-run)'s abandon is the one exception, running the final verification in place of the steps it gives up and writing no boundary. Step 10c's third pass on Vast is why: a recovery wrote *done* over a Blender install with *tooling* and *blender* unreached ([S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 4).

**Y4. The boundary list is composed at run start from the steps the run contains, in [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of)'s order, and recorded in the per-kind field beside the last-reached marker.** The full set, each present only where its step is:

| Boundary | Guarantees |
|---|---|
| **started** | the record exists; **no machine-level state is set yet**; the machine is untouched |
| **fetched** | the archive is whole at its staging path, its digest matching the published checksum; nothing is installed |
| **tooling** | **the machine-level setup-in-progress state is set** where this run is machine-changing and this is its first such step ([Y1](#31-two-classes-of-setup-run)'s rule, which sets it before the first machine-changing step whichever one that turns out to be); the unpack tool is present; the archive is unpacked into staging by that tool; nothing else is installed |
| **libraries** | the read list is installed and `ldd` re-read clean; the driver is untouched |
| **driver placed** | the driver step's work ran to its own end and the check's outcome after it is recorded; no restart has been issued |
| **restart issued** | the state is durable and the restart command was issued; the machine may be down |
| **card answering** | the check passed — on this boot, not on a reading taken in any hand-reloaded state |
| **blender** | the build answered the expected version in staging and was then published at its final name ([R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 7's order) |
| **telegram** | the role's material is placed and the desired state set |
| **done** | the final verification passed; the state (where set) is cleared and reconciliation ran |

**The one boundary written before its step rather than after it, and it is *restart issued*. Added 2026-09-27, outside any round, out of step 10c's seventh passes.** This mark is written durably just before the restart command is sent, because the command takes the connection and the machine down and nothing can be written after it — so here [T3](#8-the-restart)'s *"durable before the command is issued"* governs, and [Y3](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries)'s *"when the step it names has completed"* does not. *(Step 10c's seventh passes on Hyperstack and Sesterce found the mark written after the command, so it was never written; the recovery [T5](#8-the-restart) names then never ran.)*

**Y4a. Beside each boundary, the same durable act records whether the step performed its act or found nothing to perform — a closed set of two, decided by the test [Y1](#31-two-classes-of-setup-run) already uses. Added 2026-08-25, per Row 488.** [Design](../Design/Server%20Setup.md) marks each step of a *Nothing to prepare* run *nothing to do* rather than *done*, because *done* on a step that installed nothing claims work that never happened — and [Y3](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries)'s boundary cannot say which it was: it is written when the step completed and its verification read passed, whatever the step found.

- **The run can tell the two apart at the moment the boundary is written, and the answer is recorded rather than reconstructed.** Every step that can have nothing to do is check-first by its own rule: the tooling step installs the unpack tool *where absent* ([R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 3), the libraries step installs the `ldd` not-found list, which can be empty (item 4), the driver step is check-first outright — *"where it answers, nothing is installed and nothing is touched"* (item 5) — and the restart runs only where the check failed (item 6). So whichever side writes the boundary ([Y3](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries), [Y7](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)) knows whether the step performed an act, and writes that beside it, in the same durable act: **performed**, or **nothing to do**. The test is the act — the same test Y1 classifies runs by, applied per step — and the set is closed at two; no third mark exists to invent.
- **Readable and retained with the list, nothing second anywhere**: the mark lives in the per-kind field beside its boundary, is read over the connection under [Y6](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries), and rides the record's own retention ([Y6a](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries), `Rented-server runtime` §13 R1's term).
- **The design's third mark is not a mark, and none is invented for it.** Where the machine held no Blender and the run fetched none, the libraries step **is not composed into the run at all** — [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 4's rule, [Y4](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries)'s *each present only where its step is* — so it has no boundary and gains no per-step outcome, and writing one would record a check that never ran, which is exactly what [R4](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) and [W1](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine) forbid claiming. **What the panel's *not checked* line is composed from is the recorded composition itself**: Y4's list is composed at run start from the steps the run contains, so a recorded list holding no libraries entry is a positive record that the run contained no such step — a reading of what was composed, never an inference from an absent answer — and R4's clause is what may be said about the half. The line's wording is Design's; the two readable facts behind it are the list's composition and the run's ending.
- **[W1](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s five endings are untouched.** The marks compose the step list under whichever ending the run reached, cut-off runs included: Row 360's three kinds — done, in flight, never started — still come from the boundary and the marker, and the mark says of each *done* step which of the two things *done* was.

**Y5. Granularity is one boundary per step whose repetition is not free**, which is why the driver has three: the boundary before the restart is what a machine that never comes back is read against, and the boundary after the check is what stops a re-run ever reinstalling a driver that answers.

**Y6. The setup state, its named operation, its boundary list and its last-reached marker are readable over the connection**: reported on every health answer (V9), and answered from the operation record like any command result. **A submission refused while the state stands is told the state's own operation identity besides — A5b's refusal at the admission point, carrying the identity P1 holds — and it is the two routes above, never that refusal, that carry the boundary list and the marker. Corrected 2026-08-29 at the stage-close gate, which found the same fault here as in [S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 1:** this named **P8** as the third route, and P8's actor is *a setup run that finds the state already set*, which P2 and A11a never admit — so the route was written for a run that cannot exist, and P8 carries no boundary in any case. **The reading itself is unchanged and no route is lost**: what a refused submitter learns is now owed by the clause that issues the refusal. **That is Row 90's setup-state half answered yes** — the machine can be read for how far a half-finished setup got, by name, with the moment — on Fact 49's observation. Which Blenders and add-ons it holds is the separate reading of [Sections 5](#5-the-blender-lifecycle) and [6](#6-add-ons).

**Y6a. A *finished* setup is readable on the same terms as a half-finished one, and this is what the card is composed from. Added 2026-08-21, per Rows 355 and 356.** [Design](../Design/Server%20Setup.md) draws a card, on a connection opened long after the run, saying **that a setup happened, when it happened, and what it consisted of** — and Y6 as written promises only *how far a half-finished setup got*.

- **Nothing new is recorded. What changes is that the record is not thrown away.** The setup operation record already holds the boundary list Y4 composed at run start and the last-reached marker with its moment; **a run that finishes reaches the *done* boundary and the record then says so.** S6 clears the machine-level **setup-in-progress state** at *done* and clears nothing else — the operation record is a different thing and is not touched by that clear.
- **Its retention is now stated rather than inferred.** `Rented-server runtime` [§13](Rented-server%20runtime.md#13-retention-on-the-machine) R1 gains a term for it: **a setup operation record is retained until the next setup run on that machine replaces it at run start**, and is ended by nothing else — no age, no cap, no sweep, no free-space reading. **One per machine, so the machine holds the last setup and never a history of them.** *(Narrowed 2026-08-25 by [Y6b](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries), per Row 487: the run this term names is the ready action's, and R1's term is now per kind — a setup run of another kind replaces nothing of this record.)*
- ***That a setup happened*** is the record existing with *done* among its boundaries. ***What it consisted of*** is Y4's boundary list, which is exactly what the run contained — **so a container's card has no driver line at all**, because R3 and Fact 48 keep the driver step out of that machine's run and therefore out of its list. The card is drawn from the run rather than from a fixed pair of items.
- ***When it happened*** is the *done* boundary's moment, **on the machine's clock**, per Y3. It is shown as a moment and **never as a duration** — computing *three days ago* would subtract the machine's clock from the desktop's, which D5 forbids. **Converting the moment to the user's own zone for display is permitted and is not that subtraction**: the machine is on UTC and knows no time zone ([The rented machine](The%20rented%20machine.md)), so a zone shift uses the desktop's zone setting and reads none of its clock.
- ***Has this server ever been set up*** — Row 356's other half — **is the record's presence and nothing else.** No record, no setup. **Narrowed 2026-09-22 at Build, for [S6](#4-interruption-resumption-and-the-safe-re-run)'s abandon:** a ready action's record that ended *abandoned* is present and records a setup that did not happen, so the card reads it as *not set up* — the bullet above already says *that a setup happened* is *done* among the boundaries, and an abandon never reaches *done*. This is [Design](../Design/Server%20Setup.md)'s own reading under *The two ways out*. **And a recycled machine reads as never set up without any rule of its own**, because §2.3's installation-identity gate disqualifies every remembered value before this is read and the machine that answers is a different machine holding no record.
- **This is a fresh reading over the connection and the desktop stores none of it**, so S2's *no second home* holds unchanged and D4 is untouched: the machine still holds it. **With no connection the card is last-known, greyed and stamped**, on Row 323's rule — whose marking is composable because every reading carries its own moment and clock (`State, identity and recovery` [D5](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#3-desired-state-and-observed-state)), and whose statement for a held reading shown while nothing updates it is `Queue & History` [C7](Queue%20&%20History.md#11-one-job-two-records-two-lifetimes)'s — and never blank *(citation given its home 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R2: this named "the whole-app rule Row 323's answer already fixes" without naming where that answer lives)*.
- **The design's stated ground for its first loud case is spent and the case survives.** Y6 answered Row 90's setup-state half **yes**, so *Smoozy cannot tell what has been done to it* is no longer a state of ignorance about a machine Smoozy is talking to. **What is left of that case is exact**: no record at all, or a machine that failed the identity gate. Both are loud, and both are the same reading.

**Y6b. Which record the card reads, now that every setup act is a run: the ready action's — and the card's Telegram line is the role's own reading, never a line of the run. Added 2026-08-25, per Row 487.** [Design](../Design/Server%20Setup.md) gives the *This server* card a dedicated Telegram action and requires two things of the card at once: on a machine never prepared, it goes on saying the server is not set up while Telegram reporting is installed on it; on a machine prepared earlier, its list says Telegram is present beside what the setup did. [Y1](#31-two-classes-of-setup-run) makes the dedicated entry point a setup run like any other, so Y6a as written on 2026-08-21 — one record per machine, replaced by the next setup run of any kind — would deliver the opposite of both. **Y6a contemplated the ready action's run and no other, and that is now stated rather than left to be inferred:**

- **The setup operation record Y6a composes the card from is the ready action's** — [Section 2](#2-the-ready-action)'s machine-preparation run, and a recovery run that takes such a run over ([S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 2, whose take-over gives the recovery the taken-over run's kind). ***Has this server ever been set up* is that record's presence and nothing else**; *when* and *what it consisted of* are its *done* moment and its boundary list, per Y6a unchanged.
- **A run of any other kind replaces nothing of it.** `Rented-server runtime` [§13](Rented-server%20runtime.md#13-retention-on-the-machine) R1's term is narrowed with this clause, 2026-08-25: **one setup operation record per machine per kind of setup act** — the ready action, a Blender install, a Blender removal, an add-on install, the Telegram placement — each replaced at the start of the next run of its own kind, **except where an owning clause ties the record to a subject**, as [B8a](#53-where-a-build-goes) already does for a Blender install whose build was published (the record stands and ends with the build; an install run that published nothing falls back to the per-kind term). The Telegram placement's record is one per machine of its kind. Naming more subjects than the rows need is not done here.
- **So both of the design's sentences hold by construction rather than by rule.** On a machine never prepared, the dedicated entry point's run writes a record of its own kind and no ready-action record exists — the card still reads *never set up*, Row 356's loud case unchanged, while Telegram reporting is genuinely installed. On a machine prepared earlier, the placement replaces nothing — the card keeps the ready run's boundary list and its *done* moment.
- **The Telegram line is composed from the role's own machine-held state — [G1](#7-the-telegram-roles-material-and-its-two-entry-points)'s durable enabled-or-disabled desired state, read fresh over the connection — and never from the boundary list.** One source whichever entry point placed the material: a ready run that placed it ([R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 8) and the dedicated entry point ([G2](#7-the-telegram-roles-material-and-its-two-entry-points), [G3](#7-the-telegram-roles-material-and-its-two-entry-points)) both set the same desired state. A ready run's *telegram* boundary stays in the list as what that run did; the line about the role now is the role's reading. No file's presence is read for it (this document's own closing rule), and the desktop stores none of it (S2, D4). The line's wording, and where the design places it in the list, are Design's.
- **[G2](#7-the-telegram-roles-material-and-its-two-entry-points) and [G3](#7-the-telegram-roles-material-and-its-two-entry-points) are untouched**: what the entry point does was answered there; what its record is, and what the card reads afterwards, is this clause.

### 3.3 The worker, the driving, and the stop

**Y7. The run is driven from the desktop, one step at a time, over the connection — and that is the spec's own shape, not a limitation.** *"A connection lost part-way through stops it, and nothing resumes on its own"* is Creator-stated, and the machine cannot honour it alone because **the machine had not learnt its connection ended within the five minutes it was watched, and by `sshd_config(5)`'s documented `TCPKeepAlive` default of *"yes"* and the kernel's `tcp_keepalive_time` *"Default: 2hours"* it would not have for about two hours** (Fact 57; **the kernel document carried a site and a name and no address anywhere in `Preparation/` — read 2026-08-23 at `https://docs.kernel.org/networking/ip-sysctl.html`, where all three values hold word for word**) — **corrected 2026-08-22: this said *the machine never learns its connection ended*, which OpenSSH's own manual contradicts, the server *"will notice if the network goes down or the client host crashes"***. Two hours is no use to a step-by-step run either, so the shape below is unmoved. So:

- **Machine-side steps** (fetch, unpack, `ldd` reads, publication, read-backs) are performed by the setup worker, commanded one step at a time; each runs to its boundary and the worker then waits for the next command.
- **Elevated steps** (package installs, driver work, hand-over, the restart) are performed over the SSH command channel by the machine's own elevating command with the password on standard input ([E3](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password)); the desktop records the step's start against the run, and the boundary is written when the desktop's verification read passes. An elevated command's process is the connection's child, so a connection lost mid-step kills the step mid-flight — the boundary then honestly names the last one passed, which is Fact 49's hard-cut shape.
- **The driving side refreshes the run** at its own short cadence while the run is open. **The refresh is not a step command**: it continues at its cadence for as long as the run is open, elevated steps included, so the gap is never measured across a step that sends the worker nothing — a driver install or a long dependency chain runs for minutes without a worker command and must not end a run that is proceeding correctly. A worker whose refresh gap exceeds its declared window ends the run at its current boundary with the kind's own ending *ended at boundary N, not completed*, journalled. That is what turns a dead connection into a stopped run rather than a worker waiting forever, holding [Y9](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)'s exclusion and blocking every package commit under G21. The window's value is Build's; that it exists, and that it is measured **between refreshes** rather than against any machine-return cost, is this document's ([Section 13](#13-unresolved) item 7 records that it has never been run).

**Y7a. Every command a setup run issues has its two streams kept by the desktop, apart by stream and by step, in the order they were printed, until the next act the user asks for on that server or until Smoozy closes — a step that succeeded as much as one that failed.** **Nothing the user did not ask for empties them.** A run taken up again, after a cut or after Smoozy reopened, adds to the words it had. Acts waiting their turn in one line (Y9a) keep adding to one record, each act's words under its own steps. R1b's driver swap keeps its own words apart and never empties the setup's. The words are never written anywhere, so a reopened Smoozy starts with none. *(Changed 2026-10-30, the Creator's twenty-third notes, note 3: "Server output sometimes gets cleared for no apparent reason." It read "until the next run", and a run the user never pressed for — the next act in a line, a run taken up again, the swap — emptied the words. The build runner ruled under the standing delegation that the words stay in memory only. No auditor has seen it.)* It is the supply for the Set up card's output window (`Desktop app and delivery` M13b's second narrowing). Nothing is written to the machine for it, and a failed step's words are still S2a's, written as before and opened by *What the server said*; they are among the run's words as well. *Added 2026-10-18, Creator-stated, the thirteenth notes, note 7.* *Changed 2026-10-24, Creator-stated, the ruling on the sixteenth notes, note 1 (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's tests of the test copy/Sixteenth notes, 2026-10-21.md`): "Yes, do that, please. It should show everything the terminal on the server is producing. Is that what we're after, right? I mean, I think it is" — confirmed by the build runner as every step's printed words during setup, successful ones included, the download and the unpack keeping their figures, and nothing secret ever shown. Until then this clause read: "An elevated step's two streams are read by the desktop as they are printed, and the desktop keeps the run's words, apart by stream and by step, until the next run or until Smoozy closes. … **A machine-side step's words are the worker's own capture and reach the desktop only with a failed ending**, so the window gets nothing new while one runs." So a run that installed nothing showed nothing, which is what the Creator met on a container. `Specs/Server Setup` already promised what the server prints; the clause supplied part of it, so this is a hole closed rather than a want. No auditor has seen it.*

1. **What the desktop runs, it reads as printed.** The readings that compose the run ([R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 1, with [M3](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each)'s probe) and every elevated step go over the command channel, and each command's two streams are read as they arrive, each piece kept under the step it belongs to — **a line not yet ended included**, held as it stands and shown so until it ends *(2026-10-24, the eighteenth notes, note 5)*. **The run's words begin with those readings**, under the run's first step, and not at the moment the machine opens the run; before 2026-10-24 the readings' words were kept nowhere.
2. ~~**What the worker runs, it hands back when the step ends.** … The desktop keeps them once, at the reading that shows the step ended and before it issues anything further, so the run's order holds across both routes. **So the words are live where the transport allows**: a desktop command's as it prints, a worker step's at the first refresh after it ends. The worker's programs are short but for the unpack, which has Y7c's figure, and its capture is not made to stream.~~ **What the worker runs, it hands on as it prints.** The fetch, the unpack, the `ldd` reads, a build asked its version (R1 item 7, [B7a](#52-a-build-the-user-points-at)) and the final verification's reads are the worker's steps; each program they run is captured whole as [B1](#5-the-blender-lifecycle) already captures every Blender run, and **the capture streams**: the worker passes each stream's bytes to the supervisor as they arrive, apart, in the order the commands ran, the part of a line not yet ended included. **The worker prints its own lines on the step's streams as well, and they are the step's words like any command's**: Y7b's and Y7c's progress lines on the ordinary stream, and, where a step it runs fails, S2a's composed sentence as that step's last line on the error stream. A command step's S2a text is that command's printed words, which are already there, so nothing is added for it. The supervisor holds the step in hand's words in its running knowledge and never writes them, from the step's start until the run's next step is commanded or the next setup run opens, and **every state reading carries, with the step they belong to, the words printed since the length of each stream the desktop says it already holds** — the reading request names those two lengths, and the reading answers with what follows them, the unended line as it now stands included. The desktop keeps each piece once, in order, and keeps a step's last piece at the reading that shows the step ended and before it issues anything further, so the run's order holds across both routes. **So the words are live on both routes**: a desktop command's as it prints, a worker step's at the next refresh after it prints, which Y7d makes about a second while words are moving. A reading on a later connection carries none, and a machine program older than this carries a finished step's words at its end only, as before, or none. *Changed 2026-10-24 by the design pass for the eighteenth notes, on the build runner's rulings under the Creator's standing delegation (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the eighteenth notes, the design pass and the drawing.md`, rulings 2 and 4). Creator-stated, note 5: "I want this output to show everything the server outputs. I refuse to believe that downloading Blender, for example, is completely silent in the terminal output." No auditor has seen it.*
3. **What is never among the run's words.** ~~Smoozy's own figures and sentences — Y7b's and Y7c's counts, a fetch's refusal, a verification's finding; … and the fetch itself, which runs no program.~~ The desktop's own figures and sentences — Y7b's and Y7c's counts as the desktop shows them in a step's note, the card's statuses and its sentences; Smoozy's requests to its own program and their answers as a protocol carries them; and whatever a file transfer carries. *(Changed 2026-10-24, ruling 2 of the eighteenth notes' design pass: this read "Smoozy's own figures and sentences — Y7b's and Y7c's counts, a fetch's refusal, a verification's finding; Smoozy's requests to its own program and their answers; whatever a file transfer carries; and the fetch itself, which runs no program", which contradicted this clause's head, where a failed step's S2a words are among the run's words. What the worker prints on a step's streams, its refusal sentence and its progress lines included, is the step's words (item 2).)* Smoozy's placement of its own program before an add's setup ([R2](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of), `Servers` Q1) is the add's act and no step of the run, so its commands are outside too.
4. **Nothing secret is shown, and it is kept out by never being emitted, never by a filter** (`Security and licence` §13 G1). The words are shown as printed and nothing is taken out of them (`Desktop app and delivery` M9). What keeps each secret out is where it goes: **the machine password** rides the elevating command's standard input and is never an argument ([E3](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password)), so what is shown is that command's own prompt and never the password; **the bot token** is placed as a file by the file-transfer subsystem ([G1](#7-the-telegram-roles-material-and-its-two-entry-points), `Security and licence` §10), and no setup command names it or reads it; **no secret is ever an argument or an environment value, and no setup command prints the machine's environment or the provider's settings** (`Security and licence` §13 rules 1 and 2, [E6](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password)); **no setup command prints a host key** (rule 3). Every command string is fixed at build time ([E7](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password)), so this is a property of one closed set of commands and is tested against it.
5. **How much is kept.** Each stream whole, every step's, with no limit on the desktop: the window shows its three lines and the panel the whole. ~~**One bound, on the worker's route alone**: a step's words ride the reading whole or not at all (`Security and licence` §13 rule 5), and where the reading with them would pass `Server protocol and transfers`' largest frame, it carries instead that the step's words were not kept, and the desktop keeps that fact under the step.~~ **One bound, on the worker's route alone.** A reading carries what follows the desktop's two lengths up to the room left under `Server protocol and transfers`' largest frame, cut at a line end where one falls inside that room and otherwise at a character boundary, and the next reading carries the rest; each piece is an unaltered run of the capture's own bytes, so the capture still reaches the desktop whole and nothing is taken out of it (`Security and licence` §13 rule 5, M9). **Where a step's words would pass what the supervisor may hold in its running knowledge** — a bound Build names and records — it stops holding them; the next reading carries that the rest of that step's words were not kept, and the desktop keeps that fact under the step, after the words it already holds. *(Changed 2026-10-24 with item 2: a step's words rode one reading whole or not at all, and a step too large for one frame kept none. Words already taken now stay, so the one case that loses anything loses only the end of one step, and Design's sentence says so beneath the words. No auditor has seen it.)* The words the user then reads are Design's.

**Y7b. While the fetch runs, the worker counts the bytes it has written to the staged archive, and the run's state reading carries that count beside the archive's whole size, where the download's answer announced one.** A resumed fetch ([B4](#51-the-list-and-the-fetch)) counts what was already staged. It is the supply for the fetch step's figure on the Set up card (`Design/Server Setup § A long step`), and the desktop reads it at Y7's refresh and at no other moment — `Desktop app and delivery` M19's third route, a reading reporting its own result, and no fourth; and it is the worker's own count, never the far end's view (M20). ~~**It is not the server's output and is never put among the run's words** (Y7a).~~ **The count as the desktop shows it in the step's note is the desktop's figure and is never put among the run's words** (Y7a item 3); **beside it, the worker prints the same count as a progress line on the fetch step's ordinary stream**, about once a second while bytes arrive and once more when the fetch ends, naming the archive, how much of it is staged, the whole size where announced, and the rate — Design's words — and those lines are the step's words like any program's (Y7a item 2). It is not a boundary, nothing decides anything on it, and it need not be durable: a fetch resumed after a cut counts again from what is staged. The worker reports it no more often than the refresh cadence. *(Changed 2026-10-24, ruling 4 of the eighteenth notes' design pass, Creator-stated, note 5: "I refuse to believe that downloading Blender, for example, is completely silent in the terminal output." It read "It is not the server's output and is never put among the run's words (Y7a)." The fetch runs over the worker's own HTTPS client and no program prints, so until the worker printed its own lines the step was silent. No auditor has seen it.)* A reading from a machine program older than this carries no count, and the step shows its name alone. *Added 2026-10-18, the build runner's ruling on the Creator's fourteenth notes, note 1: "Server setup output doesn't actually output anything." It changes the machine program. No auditor has seen it.*

**Y7c. While the unpack runs, the worker feeds the staged archive to the unpacker itself and counts the bytes fed, and the run's state reading carries that count beside the archive's size, the same way and at the same cadence as Y7b.** Added 2026-10-18 by the build runner, the Creator's fourteenth notes, note 1 (*"I didn't see anything at all"*): R1 item 4 timed the unpack at about a minute against a download of seconds, so Y7b alone would leave the longest step silent. The count is of the archive read, never of files written, so it needs no fact about what the archive holds; nothing is written to the machine for it beyond the run's state, and a failed unpack still ends as before. No auditor has seen it. **The worker prints it as a progress line on the unpack step's ordinary stream the same way as Y7b's**, about once a second while the unpack runs, and those lines are the step's words (Y7a item 2). *Added 2026-10-24 with Y7b's line, the eighteenth notes, note 5. No auditor has seen it.*

**Y7d. While words are moving, the refresh runs about once a second. Added 2026-10-24 by the design pass for the eighteenth notes, ruling 4, Creator-stated, note 5: "Server output small 3-line window lags behind the actual output."** Y7's refresh, which is also the run's state reading (Y7a item 2, Y7b, Y7c), ran only at `THE_REFRESH_CADENCE`, the keepalive interval (`Servers — values` keepalive, 15 seconds), so a worker step's words and the figures arrived up to fifteen seconds late. **The desktop now refreshes once a second while words are moving** — the last reading carried new words or a moved Y7b or Y7c count, or a desktop-run command's stream delivered bytes within the last second — and **goes back to `THE_REFRESH_CADENCE` after five readings in a row bring nothing new**. A run opens at the fast cadence, so the first readings' words show at once. **Y7's gap window is measured between refreshes at either cadence**, so the faster one only shortens gaps and never ends a run; the window's value is unchanged. The worker reports Y7b's and Y7c's counts and prints their lines no more often than once a second. A refresh is one request on the connection's existing channel and writes nothing to the machine beyond what a refresh already writes. A machine program older than this answers the faster refresh as it answers any refresh. No auditor has seen it. **The second is measured from the start of the reading before, so a reading that took longer than a second is followed at once rather than by a further second; and since `Server protocol and transfers` §1.1's entry-point link stands between asks on one connection, a refresh is one command and about two round trips of the line.** *Added 2026-10-26 by the builder of the worldwide pass's setup faults, out of that pass: with the link planted and taken away on every ask, a refresh on a server 300 ms away each way took 3.6 seconds and was followed by a further second, so the card's words came in batches every 4.6 seconds at the desk and every 3 to 12 seconds on the pass, and lines between two readings never stood on the three-line window at all. At the same distance at the desk each line now reaches the card 0.4 to 1.4 seconds after it is printed. **And the screen is told the run's record off the reading already taken** where nothing has been written to the run since — one request, two readings — and **told the run as it ends**, so the last step reads done the moment it is. No auditor has seen it.*

**Y7e. A failed setup’s own reason. Added 2026-11-04, the build runner’s ruling.** The desktop holds the reason beside Y7a’s output until the next user-started setup. If both streams are empty, the panel uses it in place of *No output.* It remains Smoozy’s reason, never inserted into a server stream. The diary records the reason with the desktop run and server row; no installation identity is required, so failure before placement cannot lose its reason for lack of one.

**Y8. The user's stop (Row 113) is the durable desired state, honoured at a boundary and never by a signal.** The stop command writes *should be stopped* durably first (X8 step 2); the worker checks it at each boundary and ends there; no signal is ever sent to the worker or its children, because a setup step's children include the package system mid-transaction, and killing that is how a machine gets into the state Row 114 exists to get it out of. A stop arriving during an elevated step ends the driving side's part at once — the desktop stops issuing and closes the step's channel — and the run ends at the last boundary passed. **A restart already begun cannot be called back**: stopping then only stops the waiting, the machine still restarts, and the standing state holds the machine safe until the user picks the setup up again (the spec's own sentence, kept by P4/P7). Stopping leaves exactly what a lost connection leaves.

**Y8a. Stopping is one control offered for the whole of a run's life, and it has two outcomes — decided by whether the machine can take the durable write, never by the user choosing between two acts. Added 2026-08-23, per Row 439.** [Design](../Design/Server%20Setup.md) offers stopping *throughout* and states that a restart already begun cannot be called back.

- **Where the machine answers** — a machine-side step in flight, an elevated step in flight, or the worker waiting at a boundary for its next command — the outcome is [Y8](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop) entire: *should be stopped* written durably first, the worker ending at its next boundary, and, for an elevated step, the driving side stopping issuing and closing the step's channel so the run ends at the last boundary passed.
- **Where the machine is down, which is the restart wait ([T4](#8-the-restart)) and nothing else**, the durable write cannot be made and no request leaves at all — so the stop reaches the desktop's waiting alone. **That is the design's second clause as a property of the machine's unreachability rather than a rule written to enforce it**, which is why *cannot be called back* needs no mechanism of its own.

**And a stop made during the restart wait is not held for delivery when the machine answers.** No request leaves, so there is no pending-command record to resolve (`Server protocol and transfers` C3, and C20's rule that a sent request cannot be un-sent), and [T6](#8-the-restart) stands unchanged: the mark stands, the machine-level state stands, `Servers`' reconnect policy for that marked period goes on running, and the setup is picked up on return by [T5](#8-the-restart)'s recovery run. **The earliest end available to the user after a restart is that recovery run's next boundary**, which is the first outcome above.

**Y8b. What a stop leaves, said for the state and the interrupted job rather than for the machine alone. Added 2026-08-23, per Row 439.**

- **The machine-level state stands.** [S6](#4-interruption-resumption-and-the-safe-re-run)'s clearing set is closed at two — a run reaching *done*, and a recovery run the user asked to abandon the machine-changing work — and a stop is neither.
