Added 2026-08-21, per Row 357.** [Design](../Design/Server%20Setup.md) draws three lines and the third — *"jobs have run here, and none of them said what did the work"* — cannot be composed from one job.

- **The reading is on the machine and never on the desktop's history.** A history entry carries the server's **label** as a copy taken at submission (`Queue & History` N12) and no server identity, so the desktop cannot answer *on this machine* at all. **The machine's own finished-job records can**, they are already there, and the installation-identity gate has already established that it is the same machine.
- **It asks three questions and returns three answers, never a listing.** Does any finished job record exist; which is the most recent, by the machine's own clock; and **does any record whose recorded outcome is *finished* carry a recorded device line**, with the most recent that does. **Corrected 2026-08-31 with [W4](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)**: the third question used to be asked of the records at large, and a line printed by a run that did not finish would have been selected by it. It is asked only of jobs whose recorded outcome is *finished*, which is where W4 may read one at all. **Nothing is enumerated to the desktop** and the reading is bounded by whatever `Rented-server runtime` §13 R1 has retained.
- **The three lines fall straight out.** No finished job record — *nothing has run here yet*. At least one *finished* record carrying a line — W4's conclusion, drawn from the most recent that carries one. **Records exist and not one of them qualifies — *jobs have run here and none of them said***, which is W4's *its absence concludes nothing* stated across the set instead of about one job. **A machine whose only device lines were printed by runs that did not finish reads as this third line**, because no such line is ever selected.
- **This clause widens the *set of jobs asked*, not what may be concluded from any of them.** W4 still reads one thing from one job and still concludes nothing from an absence; **its outcome gate of 2026-08-31 is W4's own and is not loosened here.** A machine that has only ever rendered EEVEE has finished jobs and nothing to read, and the third line says so rather than reading as the first.
- **One ordering rule, because it is the only clock involved.** *Most recent* is by the machine's own clock across records the machine holds, which crosses no clocks and needs none of D5's care.

**W4. Row 308: this module reads one thing from a job whose recorded outcome is *finished* — the recorded device line — and concludes inside the spec's exact bounds. Corrected 2026-08-31, out of the repaired Blender research.** The line is Cycles' own closing report, observed in passing on the version's own stream by the render worker and recorded in the attempt record (`Rendering` L26).

**This clause read that *a stopped render never reaches it*, and that sentence was the whole ground for trusting a line found on the stream. It is false.** After a stop, **5.0 and 5.1 print the whole closing block, 5.2 prints a cancelled line and no block, and 4.5 prints nothing**; and **5.2's own published fix list calls that block on exit fake, with wrong numbers.** Label: **observed in a test**, with that version's own publication behind the last part. **So a device line observed on a run that did not finish is not evidence of anything.**

**The reading is therefore gated on the job's recorded outcome and never on the line's presence.** The device line is read only from a job whose recorded outcome is *finished* — `Queue & History` **E12**, repaired the same day on the same evidence, is the rule and this clause states no second one — and for every other ending, **a stopped run that printed a block included**, the job's devices are not known. EEVEE and Workbench establish nothing, and **the line's absence concludes nothing** — a job whose line was not observed is a job whose devices are not known, full stop.

**Where all three hold — the job's recorded outcome is *finished*, its manifest's device ask was the cards, and its recorded line names the processor alone** — this is a setup fault, said so, with the one thing Smoozy knows to do — run the ready action again — offered under [W3](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s warning since it can restart the machine.

**One dependency that reaches outside this module, named 2026-09-01 out of the repaired Blender research.** Those same three conditions are also produced by Smoozy's **own** launch line getting the device argument's name wrong: after the bare double dash an argument Cycles does not know is swallowed without a word and the render goes ahead at exit 0 on the processor (`Rendering` **L24a**). **This clause cannot tell the two apart from anything it reads**, and it would then blame the machine for Smoozy's own line and restart it. **What keeps that from happening is L24a's rule that the argument's spelling is a constant of that contract at one declaration point, never composed per job** — so a wrong spelling is one Build-time fault, caught once, rather than a condition this module could meet in the field. **This clause depends on that rule and does not restate it.** Where the job asked for the processor: nothing is said about the machine. **What the gate prevents is exact, and it is why this correction is not a wording change: as the clause stood, a fabricated closing block printed by a run that did not finish could fire a setup fault and restart a machine.**

---

## 11. Declarations

**Constraint:** `Rented-server runtime` §7.1's declarations and X11's four named things, owed by this document for the setup kind. **A1's declarations became five on 2026-08-22**, the fifth being machine-level exclusion of an operation's own kind — added at this document's own asking (§13 item 15) — and the table below carries it as its last row. Corrected 2026-08-22 at the stage-close gate, which found this line still saying four while the table said five.

| Declaration | Value |
|---|---|
| resource class | processors and the machine's memory **shared**; no card. The machine-changing run's real exclusion is the setup-in-progress state, which holds every admission at machine level (P2) and needs no card class to do it |
| priority | below the render kind; above nothing else |
| cancellable | **at a recorded safe boundary only** ([Y7](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)); never at an arbitrary moment |
| atomic or partial | **partial across steps, atomic within a step** — §9.2 test 1: the spec promises per-step outcomes (a step is reported, resumed and re-run singly) and no whole-run outcome; each step's own material moves by staging and one publication |
| exclusion | **one setup run per machine**, enforced at the admission point ([Y9](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)); per machine, never per app |

**The termination contract's four named things (X11):** which member receives the termination — **none**: the setup kind takes no signal; termination is the durable *should be stopped* honoured at the next boundary, the same no-signal shape as transfer-support's (X14). Whether at any moment or a boundary — boundary only. What cleanup is owed — the run's staged material at the locations its operation record names, swept on the next setup run's start under G20's bounded shape; the fetch staging is kept while its record says a resume is possible. *Built as a sweep at a run's completed ending rather than at the next run's start, the two paths its record names, under the name of the run that staged them through any chain of recoveries. From 2026-11-03 the staging folder itself goes with them where that leaves it empty; every run makes it again when it opens. No auditor has seen this text.* *Built 2026-11-03, the runner's ruling, the other half of this clause: **a run's start sweeps what no resume can reach**, once the run is admitted, each earlier run's two paths by its own record and nothing else (G20's bound). A run that was abandoned is swept by any run's start. **A fresh ready action sweeps every earlier run's**, because it replaces the last ready action ([Y6b](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries)) and a press takes over a stopped run this copy knows before it opens a fresh one ([S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 5), so what it finds is out of reach. A recovery's own material, named through its chain, is never swept. **A stopped run stays while a resume is possible**: a Blender install or a Telegram placement opening beside it sweeps only the abandoned. No auditor has seen this text.* **Whether the worker outlives every member of its group — yes, declared**: the setup worker remains until every external process it started has exited, or is ended only after them, which is what makes X1's leader-only recording and X9's leader-gone-means-group-gone sound for this kind.

---

## 12. What this document supplies, and to which document

**To `Rented-server runtime` — the setup kind, complete. Added 2026-08-29 at the stage-close gate, which found this section naming every document this one supplies and not the one [Section 11](#11-declarations) is written for.** §7.1's five declarations, in [Section 11](#11-declarations)'s table: resource class — the processors and the machine's memory **shared**, no card; priority — below the render kind, above nothing else; cancellable — at a recorded safe boundary only ([Y7](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)); atomic or partial — **partial across steps, atomic within a step**; and the fifth, machine-level exclusion of the kind's own operations — **one setup run per machine, never per app** ([Y9](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)), which is the declaration [Section 13](#13-unresolved) item 15 asked A1 for and A1 took on 2026-08-22. The termination contract's four named things (X11): which member receives the termination — **none**, the setup kind takes no signal; at a boundary only; cleanup being the run's staged material swept on the next setup run's start under G20, the fetch staging kept while its record says a resume is possible; **and the worker outlives every member of its group — declared yes**, which is what X1's leader-only recording and X9's leader-gone-means-group-gone need for this kind. **What [Y9](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop) requires of the admission point beyond the declaration is stated there in this document's own words** — *"A second setup submission while one run is open is refused with the open run reported"* — a report that reaches the submitter naming the run that stands in its way, which is the promise Row 111 is answered on. **That requirement is met rather than owed, from 2026-08-29:** A5a performs the exclusion as an admission rule and A5b refuses rather than queues what it and A11 hold back, so [Y9](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop) and [S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 1 cite them instead of asserting the refusal from here. And the one thing admitted at machine level while the setup-in-progress state stands is A11a's exception, [S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 2's recovery-run worker start and nothing else ([Section 13](#13-unresolved) item 14).

**To `Servers` — the restart's marks, and its half of Row 108.** The *restarting, caused by Smoozy* mark on the connected period, set before the command leaves and **cleared by exactly two things and nothing else: the machine answering, or `Servers`' reconnect policy for a restart-marked period giving up** ([T4](#8-the-restart)) — **a user who stops waiting clears nothing**, the mark and the machine-level state both stand, and the reconnect policy goes on running ([T6](#8-the-restart)); the requirement that the dropped-connection repair and the could-not-restore notification are suppressed while it stands; the *restarting* state the server reads as while it waits; and the constraint on its reconnect policy for that mark — no figure derived from any one machine's observed return cost, the observed spread being 9 to 90 seconds plus a 90-second recycle (T6). The reconnect attempt count and pauses are that document's; this one takes them as a citation.

**To `Render Settings` — the version list and the pick's identity.** The search's fresh result as (path, answered version, and whether the answer was long-term-support or unstable) per build — all three taken from the `Blender ` line and from nothing else ([B1](#5-the-blender-lifecycle)) — period-held, with [B11](#54-the-search)'s one drawn mark — candidates that did not answer, named — and its two kept but not drawn, refusals counted and renamed binaries invisible (2026-10-02); the requirement that the submission payload carry the picked build's **path and answered version** ([B13](#55-taking-a-blender-off)); the below-floor case surfacing from the version answer at install; and the add-on offer's claims ([A5](#6-add-ons)) for what the settings page may honestly show.

**To `Rendering` — the machine its launch contract requires.** The manifest's build present at its recorded path and answering its version (L14's confirmation unchanged); the add-ons installed and switched on through the machine's saved preferences per [A5](#6-add-ons)'s claim discipline and no more; and the statement that nothing in that document installs, removes or elevates — its §8 sentence, honoured from this side. **And, from 2026-10-03, the OptiX library its L24b looks for**, on Ubuntu where this document installed the driver ([R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 5's fifth act); elsewhere its absence is that clause's refusal.

**To `Dependency Upload & Remap`** — the case of driving a bare machine does not arise, per its own §1: the check needs a Blender this document put there and this document installs the package. What an add-on leaves on disk is a dependency and is that module's; this document's add-on work stops at installed-and-switched-on.

**To `Telegram` — Row 91's placement performed.** [Section 7](#7-the-telegram-roles-material-and-its-two-entry-points) whole: material placed by whichever entry point runs while Telegram is on; the dedicated entry point's no-restart conduct; replacement deliveries on the same vehicle.

**To `Queue & History`** — nothing new: this document reads its §17 live set and recorded versions and writes nothing of its own into any record that document defines. What becomes of a waiting job whose Blender was removed stays that document's.

**To `File Browser`** — the elevation flow of [Section 9](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password) as the shared mechanism Fact 27 binds at both weights: the non-interactive probe, the validate-first separation (E4), the stdin route, and the invariant that a failed operation after a successful validate forgets nothing. The browse-weight offer itself is that document's. **And the Blender table's folder link ([B12a](#54-the-search))**: a server row already in hand and one machine path, handed as the position the browser opens at, with nothing picked and nothing returned — consumed under that document's P3, which already covers a browser landing on a path directly.

**To `Desktop app and delivery`** — the fallback re-run ([Section 9b](#9b-the-user-facing-fallback-re-run)) as V10's named fallback, including the plain-start step for the condition its §22 item 15 records; G1a's or G1b's one package claim shared with the automatic trigger (corrected 2026-08-22); and the rule that an immediately previous supported server protocol keeps its control route and does not trigger repair merely because it is previous. The report notes the one-line plain-start decision left with it. The actual current-plus-previous adapter window and its inspect/stop/release/collect operations remain `Server protocol and transfers`' and are not restated here.

**To `Reporting a problem` — this module's diary-act declaration, and its told lines. Added 2026-08-31**, against that document's per-module requirement (J2, J2a, J3, J4a), which no clause of this one carried and which no reader of this one met any signal of. A declaration is a fixed list: the acts below are the whole of it, an act failing any of J2a's three tests is not declared, and a module writes only what its declaration names.

**The identities every line below carries**, once, so that no line composes them at report time: the desktop run identity of the run that wrote it (`Reporting a problem` U7); the server row the act was performed on; the installation identity the machine answered under (§2.3's carrying rule), so that a line about a recycled machine can never read as a line about the machine that replaced it; and the moment on the **desktop** clock, stated as that clock (D5). **The lines are per act the user asked for and never per setup run** — the quick path's two ticked options run as one run ([R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of), which composes the Blender publication into the ready action's own sequence) and write one line each.

- **The ready action ran.** Smoozy's own act; the spec promises *the machine made able to render* by a single action and [W1](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s ending is what the user is told; one per action asked for. **Fixed form:** the act's name, and which ending the run reached, from a closed set of six — [W1](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s five statements, plus *stopped part-way* for a run that ended at a boundary without completing. **Written when the run reaches that ending with the desktop driving it** ([Y7](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)); a run the desktop lost is carried by the first told line below when its ending is read back, and is never guessed at. **A recovery run carrying an interrupted ready action on ([S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 2, [T5](#8-the-restart)) is that same act and writes no second line**, and nothing is written per step, per boundary, per elevated command or per retry.
- **A Blender was installed.** Smoozy's own act; the spec names installing one as an outcome and what the machine then holds is read back and shown ([B10](#54-the-search)); one per install asked for. **Fixed form:** the act's name; the version the build answered in staging ([B1](#5-the-blender-lifecycle)); which of [B8a](#53-where-a-build-goes)'s two routes placed it — the published listing, or a build the user pointed at; and whether it was published or **declined at the version answer** ([B7a](#52-a-build-the-user-points-at)). Never one line per resumed byte range of the fetch ([B4](#51-the-list-and-the-fetch)).
- **A Blender was taken off.** Smoozy's own act; the spec gives the user the removal and the machine's list afterwards is where it shows; one per removal asked for. **Fixed form:** the act's name; the version the removed build answered; and which of [B15](#55-taking-a-blender-off)'s two routes performed it — the folder deletion, or the machine's own software manager.
- **An add-on was installed.** Smoozy's own act; *Smoozy offers, it does not install unasked*, and what the module then says — installed and switched on — is the spec's own sentence ([A5](#6-add-ons)); one per add-on the user took the offer on. **Fixed form:** the act's name; the add-on's own name as the offer named it; which of [A1](#6-add-ons)'s two routes supplied it — by name from a repository already on the build's list, or a copy from the user's own computer; the Blender version whose settings folder it was installed into ([A6](#6-add-ons)); and what [A5](#6-add-ons)'s read-back concluded. Never one line per file inside the packed directory ([A2](#6-add-ons)).
- **Telegram's piece was placed by its own action.** Smoozy's own act; the spec gives it an action of its own for a machine set up earlier, and the *This server* card's Telegram line is where it shows ([Y6b](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries)); one per use of the dedicated entry point. **Fixed form:** the act's name, and that the desired state was set to enabled ([G2](#7-the-telegram-roles-material-and-its-two-entry-points)). **Where the ready action places the same material as its step 8 ([R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 8), that is a step inside the act the user asked for and writes no line of its own.**

**The told lines this document writes — two, declared so that the absence of the rest is a decision** (J4a). Each carries J4's four things and nothing computed across two clocks: the machine's own moment on the machine's clock, the journal sequence number the thing arrived at, the installation identity it was read under, and the server row.

- **The machine kind and the distribution family, at every setup run** ([M1](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each), [M1a](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each)) — the reading J4 already names as this document's, written at the moment of the reading, once per run, and carrying the two answers and nothing else.
- **A setup run's own recorded ending, where the desktop reads it back rather than driving the run to it** — [S1](#41-what-a-part-finished-setup-reports) and [S2](#41-what-a-part-finished-setup-reports)'s fresh reading on a later connection, or [S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 2's take-over. One per run, carrying the ending's own kind from the closed set the record holds. **This is what a setup cut off part-way leaves in the diary**, the act line above never having been written for it.

**Nothing else this module reads becomes a told line, and the rejections are named because each is a reading a builder could mistake for one.** The card-presence scan and the driver's own answer ([R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) items 1 and 5), read up to three times inside one run; `ldd`'s not-found list and its re-read (item 4); the search's per-candidate version answers ([B10](#54-the-search), [B11](#54-the-search)), one per build the machine holds — **and the Blender version's told line is `Rendering`'s at the launch confirmation, per J4, never this document's search**; the add-on read-back listings ([A5](#6-add-ons)); the boundary list and its last-reached marker read over the connection ([Y6](#32-the-recorded-safe-boundaries)), which are per step; and [Section 9b](#9b-the-user-facing-fallback-re-run)'s package inspection, which [F1](#9b-the-user-facing-fallback-re-run) step 4 keeps inside the module because no spec grants the user a view of it. The desktop's read-forward of the event journal is not a source of told lines at all (J4a).

**What no line above carries, on J3's bound.** No machine path; no tool's captured words — [S2a](#41-what-a-part-finished-setup-reports) keeps those on the machine and `Security and licence` §13 rule 5 governs what may be done with them; no password or any part of one, and no statement that one was held; no boundary name, no exit status, no package name, no counted duration. Every value above is drawn from a closed set the cited clause already fixes, and nothing is composed at run time.

**To the architecture runner, as seams rather than supplies:** [Section 13](#13-unresolved) item 10, the one still open. Items 8, 9, 14, 15 and 16 were routed the same way and were closed at the Architecture seam close; their entries in [Section 13](#13-unresolved) record how. The last three, each in a line:

- **Item 14** — `Rented-server runtime` §9 P2 and §7.2 A11 are written absolute, and this document requires one exception to both: the recovery run's worker start, made while the machine-level state stands ([Y1](#31-two-classes-of-setup-run), [S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 2). **Closed: A11a is that field, and P2 points at it.**
- **Item 15** — §7.1's declarations carried no per-kind machine-level exclusion, and [Y9](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop) declared a fifth thing they did not hold. **Closed: A1 now takes five and this document's §11 table carries the fifth.**
- **Item 16** — `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 4's Build-side fix, the supervisor reaping the workers it starts, is carried here as **a requirement of this document** rather than as somebody else's parked item: without it, a defunct group reading as live on a machine whose supervisor is up leaves [S5](#42-the-recovery-run)'s refusal with no route out that this document can define. **Closed: X9a states the wait from that document's side.**

---

## 13. Unresolved

Each entry names what is not established, what breaks, and what holds without it. **None of them stopped this document**; [Section 14](#14-moments-that-reached-the-creator) records what was checked.

**The Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 reaches this list, and it is applied once here rather than repeated item by item. Added 2026-09-02.** There will be **no further Blender research and no further rented machine for answering a question**. *(Build may rent a machine to run the app on, ruled the same day; that is a different act and it revives nothing here.)* **So an item below whose settlement needs a rented machine or a further research pass is permanently unresolved rather than pending, and is to be read as a silence to work around rather than as work that is coming.** Those items are **1** (Fact 81 — it wants a rental whose firmware is EFI with Secure Boot on, and none of the seven this project rented had one), **2** (Fact 55's dependency-check clause, never posed on any machine), **7** (the command-gap window, which wants a machine and about two hours of it), **13** (more boxes on more days, which is the whole of the item), **18** (a card newer than anything this project has held), **19** (the frontend lock's bounded wait, whose value is documented nowhere this project may read and which wants a machine to watch), **24** (an add-on carrying a compiled part across the shared folder at the Python change, which is a research question), and the two halves of **4** that are not Fact 56. **Item 25 was already written that way on 2026-09-02 and needs nothing further.** **Two more were added to this list on 2026-09-02 at the stage-close gate repair, both of which had gone on ending *Answerable, and free*: item 17**, whose snap half wants one Ubuntu machine with snapd that this project does not have — `Facts.md` Fact 110 had already been written *"permanently unresolved rather than pending"* and this page was the last copy reading the other way — **and item 5**, whose documented repair sequence wants any Ubuntu machine and is the same shape. *"Free"* and *"no rental"* do not make a machine appear; this project has one computer and it is the Creator's Mac.

**What the ruling does not reach, and what must not be written off beside them.** **Item 3** — an actual extension package has never been fetched — is a fetch this project can perform at no cost and with no machine, and it is unrun rather than unreachable. **Fact 56, the third residue of item 4, is ANSWERED BY READING, 2026-09-02** — the marketplace's own published documentation was read, in the direction [A3](#6-add-ons) already stood, and A3 needed no change. *(This read *is answerable now by reading* until later the same day, the answer having landed in `Facts.md` and in the companion first.)* **What survives is a narrower residue**, not the question: whether an individual add-on additionally holds a seller-issued key inside its folder, neither permitted licence forbidding a runtime check of the add-on's own. **The register's binding condition stands over that residue**: the question was raised by an agent from its own knowledge and **must be reworded blind** before anyone is asked. Nobody was asked anything for the reading; a publication was read. ~~**Item 5** is its own case and is written out at the item.~~ *(Struck 2026-09-02 at the stage-close gate repair: item 5 is not its own case. What it wants is a machine, free or not, and this project has none — it now carries the permanence line with item 17.)* **Item 11's macOS half** is the Creator's own Mac and costs nothing; its Windows half is the standing Windows list, for which there will be no computer. **Item 12** turns on time passing rather than on equipment. **And items 21 and the closed entries are construction rather than fact** — settled by writing, not by observing.

1. **Fact 81 — Secure Boot — is unposable today**: none of the seven machines rented had it, and it justifies no rental of its own. **Breaks:** [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of)'s driver step on such a rental — module signing could fail or demand a human at the console, which Smoozy cannot do over SSH. **What holds:** [R9](#25-secure-boot-and-the-machine-smoozy-cannot-make-ready)'s clause — the machine is one Smoozy cannot make ready and the action says so with the machine's words; Row 88 stays answered with the clause named.
2. **Fact 55's dependency-check clause — what a restart Smoozy issues does to a reading in flight — was never posed on any machine.** That fact's own *Not covered* line names it as the one clause still open; the render half and the transfer half are both answered. **Breaks:** [T7](#8-the-restart)'s sentence that a reading dies with its worker, and [W3](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s warning for a reading in flight — if a reading can survive a shutdown in a state that is neither finished nor gone, its operation record having no outcome is not the whole story, and Row 109 closes with this named. **What holds:** reconciliation's outcome does not depend on the manner of death, so a reading whose group is gone gets its disposition either way; and the render half is observed rather than assumed — two real machine restarts with a render in flight, giving both interruption shapes at a frame's path (about 92% nothing, about 8% an unmarked partial under the frame's real name), which is what makes file presence unreadable as success.
3. **Fact 54's extension-package gap: an actual extension package has never been fetched by this project — only the listing.** **Breaks:** [A1](#6-add-ons)'s fetched-add-on route is exercised end to end by nobody; a package that arrives in a shape the from-file install refuses would surface at first use. **What holds:** the listing's reachability, the from-file route for user copies (observed end to end), and [A5](#6-add-ons)'s judge-by-read-back, which catches a failed install whatever caused it.
4. **Three add-on residues, all inherited and all named where they bite:** the packed-copy direction was observed Linux-to-Mac, the reverse of Smoozy's ([A2](#6-add-ons)); an older-style switched-off copy is visible to no route this project established, so [A6](#6-add-ons)'s install can land on a copy the listing could not show — the ruled-on cost of trusting the listing; and **Fact 56 (licence material inside a copy) is ANSWERED BY READING, 2026-09-02** — the marketplace's own support pages permit code products only under GPL or MIT, both allowing redistribution and modification, and instruct that a compliant folder carry a `LICENSE` file and a notice at the head of every Python file, **so a copy carries licence text and a copyright notice rather than an entitlement**; [A3](#6-add-ons) needed no change and now cites the reading, and the residue that survives is narrower than the question was — whether an individual add-on additionally holds a seller-issued key of its own, on one marketplace's rules on one date. **The register's blind-rewording warning stands for the day that residue is put to a person; nobody was asked anything and a publication was read.** *(This read *unanswerable here and must be reworded blind before anyone is asked* until 2026-09-02; the answer had landed in `Facts.md` and in the companion the same day and this entry was not brought along.)* **Breaks:** for each, what the module *says* rather than what it does; the claims are already cut to installed-and-switched-on.
5. **The repair operation [S7](#43-why-each-step-is-safe-to-run-again) now names — `dpkg --configure -a` then `apt-get --fix-broken install` — has never been run by this project on any machine.** It is taken from dpkg's own manual and from the instruction apt itself prints on an interrupted transaction, both quoted at S7, rather than from an observation. The one thing this project did observe was the **forced** form (`dpkg --remove --force-all` plus fix-broken, Fact 48's container case), which S7 now forbids on dpkg's own warning that it may break the whole system; **that correction was made on 2026-08-20 and it removed the only observation this entry ever had.** **Breaks:** a machine whose package state the documented repair cannot fix — the step then fails honestly and repeatedly rather than making anything worse, which is the direction Row 114's promise needs. **What holds:** the check-first driver order, which is independent; the busy-before-broken rule ([S7b](#43-why-each-step-is-safe-to-run-again)), which keeps the repair away from a package system that was never damaged; and the refusal to signal setup steps, which is what makes mid-transaction kills rare here. ~~**Answerable:** free, on any Ubuntu machine with no card and no rental — interrupt a package transaction, run the sequence, read what it says.~~ **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02, on the same ground as item 17.** That is what it would take and this project has no such machine; *"free"* names the cost of one nobody has, and no further rental will be made to answer a question. **This is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** **Guard — the documented sequence must never be re-labelled *observed*, and the forced form must never come back** on the reasoning that the documented one is untested: dpkg's own warning is why S7 forbids it. **What holds is written above and does not weaken** — the step fails honestly and repeatedly rather than making anything worse, which is the direction Row 114's promise needs.

    **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED FOR THIS STAGE, 2026-09-02, and it is not the rental ruling that leaves it open.** The *Answerable* sentence above is exact and stays: the run needs no card, no rental and no money. **What it needs is an Ubuntu machine, and this project runs none** — every Linux box it has ever held was a rental, and the Creator's Mac has no hypervisor and no container runtime installed, installing one being the Creator's own act (`Rented-server runtime` [Section 16](Rented-server%20runtime.md#16-unresolved) item 13). The Creator ruled on 2026-08-31 that no further machine will be rented to answer a question, so **the free run has nowhere to happen while this stage is open**. It is left open rather than struck because **Build has a machine by construction** and can take it there in minutes; it is written permanent for this stage so that nobody schedules it here.

    **The label is the silence-removal and must not be quietly upgraded.** What [S7](#43-why-each-step-is-safe-to-run-again) claims is exactly what dpkg's own manual and apt's own printed instruction say, and **nothing more** — the sequence has never been run by this project on any machine, and the one thing this project did observe was the forced form, which S7 now forbids. **A builder who runs the sequence once and finds it works has established that it worked once, on one machine, in one package state.** That does not make the clause observed, does not close this item, and must not be written up as though it did; what would close it is the failing case, which is the case S7's *fails honestly and repeatedly rather than making anything worse* is written for.
6. **NARROWED AGAIN 2026-08-31, and the separation half is spent for the observed case: the wrong-password refusal is told apart, and a password that has stopped working is erased rather than kept.** `Security and licence` §13 rule 5 carries a **Creator-stated exception, dated 2026-08-25**, reaching the elevation tool alone: its wrong-password ending `sudo: 1 incorrect password attempt` is matched as an **exact observed constant**, which is matching rather than mining. On that match `Security and licence` §4's invariant fires rather than withholds, and its §2 item 3 erasure — *the password ceasing to work* — is performed, so **Row 117's promise that a password which stops working is forgotten rather than retried is kept** on the release and machine kind the strings were observed on, and the break stated below is spent there. **What is left is the exception's own bound**: the not-an-administrator message names the host and is never matched, and a release or machine kind whose wording was never observed matches nothing — so an unmatched refusal keeps the password, reports the step refused, and the break below stands for those cases and no others. The strings are observed on one rental (`sudo` 1.9.15p5 under Ubuntu 24.04's stock sudoers, 2026-08-22) and documented nowhere this stage reads; `Security and licence` §14 items 15 and 17 own that limit, and the virtual-machine kind under a non-root login is still unposed. [E4](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password) and [W1](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine) carry the correction. **This entry was written on 2026-08-22, before the ruling, and stated the wider break for six days after it.** ~~The 2026-08-22 entry follows, unchanged.~~ **Narrowed 2026-08-22: the probe and the validate form have now both been run on a rental, and what remains is the separation alone.** On machine A of [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md), under a purpose-made non-root login with Ubuntu's stock sudoers: `sudo -n -v` returned `sudo: a password is required` at exit 1; `sudo -S -v` with the right password returned **exit 0** having run no command; and the credential cache is **15 minutes** on that image, which is what the tool's own manual at the release that image carries documents (`sudo(8)` 1.9.15p5, read 2026-08-23; **corrected 2026-08-23 — this said *rather than the manual's five*, which was the Creator's Mac's page at 1.9.17p2**) and which is still read per machine rather than assumed from any one page. **So the *never been run on a rental* half of this item is spent.** **The separation half stands, and its ground has changed from cannot to may not.** The three refusals **are** distinguishable word for word — `sudo: 1 incorrect password attempt`, `sudo: a password is required`, and `Sorry, user smoozynoadmin may not run sudo on <host>.` — all at exit 1. **`Security and licence` §13 rule 5 forbids mining that output for a cause**, and the third message **names the host**, so matching it as a constant would break on the next machine. **The break below is therefore unchanged in substance and better founded**: the password is kept, and Row 117's promise is weaker for it. [E4](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password) carries both corrections. ~~The original entry follows.~~ **A failed validate does not say which of two things happened, and the elevation probe and the validate form have never been run on a rental in any case.** `sudo -n` and `sudo -v` are documented (read 2026-08-19, [Sources](Sources/Server%20Setup.sources.md)) and `sudo` itself was observed present on the rentals; the validate-first separation ([E4](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password)) is a documented mechanism, not an observed one, and a rental shipping a different or older `sudo`, or a policy without cached credentials, changes the caching but not the separation. **What the order does separate is the operation's result from the authentication step's; what it does not separate is a wrong password from an elevation the login is refused** — the manual's own EXIT VALUE names both, and neither the exit number nor the tool's words may be mined to tell them apart (`Security and licence` §13 rule 5). **Breaks:** under `Security and licence` §4's invariant the password is therefore **not** forgotten in that case, so a password that has genuinely stopped working is kept and the user meets a repeating failure until they clear it themselves — which is §14 item 15's own named break, now arriving on this document's **documented** behaviour rather than only on a rental whose elevating command has no validate form at all. Row 117's promise that a password which stops working is forgotten rather than retried is weaker for it, and the weakening is written down here rather than papered over. **What holds:** the invariant's safe direction — a good password is never discarded, and a failed operation after a successful validate forgets nothing; the step is reported **refused** with the machine's own words and reaches [W1](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s Refused ending; and the stdin route, which was fixed upstream.
7. **The setup worker's command-gap window ([Y7](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)) has never been run.** It exists because the machine does not learn its connection ended for about two hours — the kernel's `tcp_keepalive_time` *"Default: 2hours"* under `sshd_config(5)`'s `TCPKeepAlive` *"yes"*, and the observation ran 5 min 10 s (Fact 57; **corrected 2026-08-22: this said *never learns*, and the manual of the observed software says the server does notice**; **the kernel document's address supplied 2026-08-23 — `https://docs.kernel.org/networking/ip-sysctl.html`**) — and a worker waiting forever would block [Y9](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)'s exclusion and every package commit (G21). **Breaks:** a window too short ends a run the user was still driving between slow steps — costing a recovery run, never harm; too long delays the exclusion's release. The value is Build's; the mechanism is one number in one place.

    **At a learned session cap of one, Y7's refresh and `Server protocol and transfers` [T5](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#62-concurrency-and-one-admission-point-rather-than-one-per-server) cannot both hold through an elevated step. Added 2026-09-27, out of Build step 10c's eighth pass.** Y7 has the refresh go on through elevated steps; T5 gives a machine whose learned cap is one a single lease, and the elevated command holds it for its whole length. **So on such a machine an admin step longer than the window holds the only session, no refresh can go out beside it, and the window ends the run — costing a recovery run, never harm**, which is this item's own stated cost. Every machine on record sat at the default of ten (`Facts.md` Fact 112, as `What Build inherits` carries it), so this is a residue and not the ordinary case. **Above one, nothing in T5 stops a second command beside a running one**: T5 reserves a slot for commands there and does not say one command at a time, so the refresh goes out on its own session beside the admin step, which is what Y7 asks.
8. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close: `Job files and outputs` L6 now names five areas with the Blender area as the fifth, and N1 names its paths as this document's, alongside the staging path.** The original entry follows, unchanged. **The Blender area is a path this document composes and a fifth sibling under the root, and two closed enumerations do not admit it**: `Job files and outputs` N1 names only the staging path as composed elsewhere, and its 1.2 closes the root at four areas. A seam of the same shape as the stage's store-inventory seams; the tally's one home is `Open Threads`. **Breaks:** a machine-side layout built to 1.2 as it stands has nowhere for a Blender install to go, and with it Rows 92, 93 and 95. **What holds:** everything about the area's conduct — same mount, publication by rename, never over a folder in use — which is what the owning document needs in order to name it.
9. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close: `Job files and outputs` M1 carries the build path as a field and M1a states it as B13's *path beside version*.** The original entry follows, unchanged. **The manifest carries the picked build's path beside its version in this document's matching rule ([B13](#55-taking-a-blender-off)), and `Job files and outputs` M1's field list does not name it.** Same shape as `Rendering`'s D9 and reporting-settings seams. **Breaks:** without the field, the launch has only a version, and two builds of one version are indistinguishable at launch — Row 96's match degrades to version-only and L14 cannot tell substitution within a version. **What holds:** L14's confirmation and N10's version, both unchanged; the warning still fires on version match, merely wider than it need be.
10. **The dedicated Telegram entry point on a never-prepared machine places the package ([G3](#7-the-telegram-roles-material-and-its-two-entry-points)), and whether that placement's elevated container step belongs inside an action the spec describes as "adds Telegram's piece and does nothing else" is a reading this document took rather than found written.** **Breaks:** nothing mechanical — the alternative is an entry point that refuses unprepared machines, which the spec does not say either. Recorded for the runner; the no-restart and no-other-setup promises hold on both readings.
11. **Windows and macOS are untried for every operation this module performs on the user's own computer** — reading the user's Blender settings folder for an add-on copy ([A2](#6-add-ons)), and taking a pointed-at build or add-on from disk. The machine-side half is all Linux and all observed or labelled. **Breaks:** the local pack-up on those systems rests on the research's Mac observations and on nothing for Windows; a wrong local read fails at the machine's read-back, loudly. 
12. **Fact 52's remainder after the 2026-08-19 reading:** one day's agreement between the stable channels and the downloads; the frequency over time is unknown, and installing through a named shop-front channel was still never run (nothing rests on it). **The stale-entry trap in the builder's daily listing** is recorded at [B6](#51-the-list-and-the-fetch) so nothing later reads that listing without reading the file moment. **2026-10-02: read again, and the unfinished half resolved** at [B5](#51-the-list-and-the-fetch) — the release folders held no unfinished build on either day, so unfinished builds moved to the build service ([B2](#51-the-list-and-the-fetch), [B4](#51-the-list-and-the-fetch)). **What is still not established, and stays a stop for whoever builds it:** what an unfinished build prints when asked its version — [B1](#5-the-blender-lifecycle)'s *documented and untested* — and so whether it gives [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 7 the version it was fetched as. Where it does not, item 7 fails the step in the machine's own words, and nothing here loosens that.
13. **Every claim about a rented machine in this document is one to nine boxes on one to four days, across three sellers. Widened 2026-08-22** — [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md) added nine rentals on three sellers, two operating-system families and two machine kinds, and several claims here now rest on more than one box. **The caution below is unchanged and is the point of the entry**: more boxes is not a description of rented machines. The container capability set is Docker's default and a provider running `--privileged` would answer Fact 48 differently; Fact 47's list is per image and cannot be settled; Fact 6's housekeeping-over-days half and Fact 45's flatpak, non-root-versus-renamed-binary and container/network-mount/home-directory cases are all named open in the register and inherited here unchanged. **Breaks:** nothing named — every rule is written per kind and the untrusted readings are called out as traps. Recorded so no later document reads a figure here as a description of rented machines.
14. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close: `Rented-server runtime` A11a carries the exception with the one field it needs — the operation identity the standing state names — and P2 is corrected to point at it. Nothing else is admitted, reconciliation included.** The original entry follows, unchanged. **This document requires one exception to two absolute rules in a closed document, and neither carries a field for it.** `Rented-server runtime` §9 P2 holds reconciliation and every machine-level admission back while the setup-in-progress state stands, and §7.2 A11 says the same at the admission point; [Y1](#31-two-classes-of-setup-run) declares an exception to both, and its scope is exactly one thing — **the recovery run's worker start** ([S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 2), a worker start made while the state stands. The run's own steps need no exception: A4 gates worker starts, and a setup run's worker was started before the state was set. **Breaks:** a builder implementing A11 literally refuses the recovery run's worker; the state is then never cleared, and Rows 106, 112 and 114 fail together — the setup never carries on where it left off, what it managed can never be added to, and the machine admits nothing for good. **What holds:** the exception's shape, which is what the owning document needs in order to carry it — one worker start, by the operation the state itself names after the take-over, and nothing else admitted at machine level. Recorded and routed rather than repaired: this document does not edit a closed one.
15. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close: `Rented-server runtime` A1 now takes five declarations, the fifth being machine-level exclusion of an operation's own kind, enforced by counting admitted operations of that kind. The contended-resource list stays closed at three, which is what stopped this being expressible before.** The original entry follows, unchanged. **[Y9](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)'s one-run-per-machine exclusion is declared against a mechanism with no field for it.** `Rented-server runtime` A1 closes what an owning document declares at four things and closes the contended resources at the cards, the processors and the machine's memory; A5 admits an operation when every resource its class names is available under the exclusion rule. The setup kind's class is *shared, no card* ([Section 11](#11-declarations)), which admits any number of setup runs, and the only machine-level exclusion that document defines is A11's setup-in-progress state, bolted on for one thing. **Breaks:** Row 111's one-step-at-a-time is delivered by nothing for additive runs — an add-on install and a removal submitted at once, or two add-on installs from two entry points, set no state under [Y1](#31-two-classes-of-setup-run) and are both admitted, leaving only the serialisation [A8](#6-add-ons) says Smoozy cannot rely on, Blender's own add-on store lock, which does not cover a removal at all. **What holds:** the machine-changing case, which the setup-in-progress state holds by itself. **Why it is not expressed in the mechanism as it stands:** that would mean declaring a machine-level resource the setup kind takes exclusively, and A1's contended list is closed at three, of which the processors and the memory are shared with the render kind — declaring either exclusive would exclude renders as well, which is not what Y9 says.
16. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, and it was already half closed before this entry knew it: `State, identity and recovery` §5.3 step 6 requires the supervisor to wait on every worker it starts, and `Rented-server runtime` X9a now states that requirement from its own side and marks §16 item 4's rider as no longer Build's. S5's refusal has a route out that costs the user nothing and needs no provider.** The original entry follows, unchanged. **The defunct-entry hole has no unblock this document can define while the supervisor is up, and the route out is a reap this document requires of `Rented-server runtime`.** [S5](#42-the-recovery-run)'s refusal is safe in direction, but where a dead setup group reads as live on a machine whose supervisor never restarts, nothing defined here clears the state: the group cannot end, because it is already gone; no restart is issued, because [T1](#8-the-restart) issues one only as part of a setup step that needs it and the setup is precisely what is being refused, and on a container none is issued at all; and [Section 9b](#9b-the-user-facing-fallback-re-run) F1 step 3's plain start fires only where the supervisor does not answer. `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 4's Build-side fix — **the supervisor reaping the workers it starts** — is carried as a requirement of this document and routed to the runner in [Section 12](#12-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document). **Breaks:** without it the user meets a machine that admits nothing at all — no render, no reading, no derivative, no re-run of the interrupted job — with only two acts left and both outside the app: a reboot from the provider's interface, which *servers and keys only, never providers* forbids and which Row 283's exception exists to prevent, or destroying the rental. Rows 114, 106, 90 and 283 all bear on it. **What holds:** the refusal's direction — the reading can report a dead group live and never a live one dead, so nothing here ever starts a second run beside a live one; and the ordinary case, where the worker ends its run at a boundary and the group genuinely ends.
17. **The snap half of a manager removal against a render already running from that build is not established; the package-manager half is answered by the standard and needs no machine.** A package removal unlinks, and POSIX settles what that does to a process holding the file — `unlink()`, The Open Group Base Specifications Issue 8, read 2026-08-20 at `https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/9799919799/functions/unlink.html`: *"If one or more processes have such a reference to the file when the last link is removed, the link shall be removed before unlink() returns, but the removal of the file contents shall be postponed until there are no such references to the file."* That is the same rule behind [B14](#55-taking-a-blender-off)'s folder-deletion observation — permitted at once, the render carrying on to a normal end, all four versions, on Linux and on a Mac. **What is open is the snap route**, where the build is a read-only squashfs mount rather than ordinary files (Canonical's own *System snap directory*, read 2026-08-20 at `https://snapcraft.io/docs/explanation/how-snaps-work/system-snap-directory/`, giving the mount line as `squashfs (ro,…)`) and removal must unmount it; nothing this project has read or run covers what that does to a render in flight, and no answer is guessed at here. **Breaks:** the warning would be wrong about a job in flight on the snap route, in the one sentence read aloud to the user deciding — Row 96's truthful warning. **What holds:** the warning's predicate reaches both routes ([B14](#55-taking-a-blender-off)) so no removal happens quietly; the waiting-job half is unaffected; and the snap route says the render-in-flight outcome is not known rather than borrowing the other route's answer. ~~**Answerable, and free:** one Ubuntu machine with snapd, any hardware, no card and no rental — install the Blender snap, start a long render from it, issue `snap remove`, record whether the removal is refused and whether the render survives.~~ **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02.** That run is what it would take and **this project does not have such a machine**: its only computer is the Creator's Mac, and the Creator ruled on 2026-08-31 that there will be no further rental to answer a question. *"Free"* names the cost of a machine nobody has. **`Facts.md` Fact 110 has said this since 2026-08-31** — *"So the snap half is permanently unresolved rather than pending"* — and this entry was the last copy still reading as though the run were coming. **This is not a pending item and no reader may leave a space in the code or the record for its answer.** **Guard — *not established* must never be quietly recovered as *the render survives*, on the strength of the package-manager half above**, which is settled by a standard that says nothing about unmounting a read-only mount. **What holds is written above and does not weaken**: B14's predicate reaches both routes so no removal happens quietly, and the snap route says the outcome is not known rather than borrowing the other route's answer.
18. **What the distribution's own driver-detection route installs on a card newer than anything this project has held is not established.** [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 5 was corrected on 2026-08-20 to stop naming a flavour, because NVIDIA documents that neither flavour serves every card; Ubuntu's page for `ubuntu-drivers` stops its own example listing at driver 550 and nowhere states that the tool picks the open flavour correctly for Blackwell. **The item stays open and one downstream worry beside it is removed — 2026-08-22.** [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md) ran **both** Blender versions on a **Blackwell RTX 5090** under driver `595.71.05`, on both backends, at one card and at two, with **no CUDA toolkit installed** and no failure of any kind. **So Blender itself has no difficulty with such a card**, and what is still unestablished is narrower than it was: **what the distribution's own detection route installs for it.** On those boxes the seller injected the driver and no detection route ran, so the question this item asks was not posed. **Breaks:** on such a card the installed driver fails its own check and the machine is reported not ready — Row 88's promise degrades to a refusal rather than to a wrong render. **What holds:** the check-then-restart order, which reads the driver's own answer and never the package manager's word (Fact 80), so a wrong flavour is loud rather than quiet; and [W4](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s refusal to claim the machine renders on its cards.
19. **The bounded wait a package step gives the frontend lock ([S7b](#43-why-each-step-is-safe-to-run-again)) has no established value.** `DPkg::Lock::Timeout` appears in apt's own source and in neither `apt.conf(5)` nor `apt-get(8)`, so its behaviour at a non-zero value is documented nowhere this project may read. **Breaks:** too short and a machine still finishing its startup updates is reported busy when waiting a little longer would have served; too long and a genuinely stuck lock delays the run's honest failure. Neither direction damages a machine. **What holds:** the failure direction — a step blocked by the lock is reported busy and never repaired, so the forbidden compound cannot occur at any value.
20. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close: `The shape` §11 now says the user-facing fallback re-run goes through the same package owner and the same trusted release selection as every automatic path.** The original entry follows, unchanged. **This document's scope line is [The shape](The%20shape.md#11-server-setup) §11's own wording, and [Section 9b](#9b-the-user-facing-fallback-re-run) goes past it.** Added 2026-08-22, when a widened wording that had been standing in the scope line was restored to §11's. §11 assigns *"user-facing fallback re-run for supervisor-independent package repair and replacement"* and says nothing about how that re-run is admitted; [F1](#9b-the-user-facing-fallback-re-run) step 2 and [F3](#9b-the-user-facing-fallback-re-run) put it through **the same exclusive package owner and the same trusted release selection as every automatic path** — `Rented-server runtime` G1a's kernel-held lock — or G1b's desktop-side exclusive create where no machine-side program exists to hold one (corrected 2026-08-22) — acquired before any phase changes, and G26 to G31's signed manifest, exact target tuple and digest instead of a version label. **Why that is nevertheless true of this document as it stands:** F3 states outright that the fallback has no package-transaction exception, and without the clause a user-triggered re-run would be a second unlocked writer of the same phase record and a second route by which an unverified artifact could reach the machine — a privilege the shape's own *"Server Setup's user-facing re-run is the fallback if automatic repair fails"* never grants it. **Owner:** `The shape`, which is where document ownership is recorded and which this document may not edit; §11's list is to be made to agree, and this is a seam rather than a defect. **Breaks:** nothing in the mechanism; what is unresolved is that [Section 9b](#9b-the-user-facing-fallback-re-run) answers no row of its own and stands entirely on the shape's assignment, so a reader taking §11 as the complete assignment finds F1 step 2's admission and F3 unassigned — and, reading §11 alone, would be free to build the fallback as its own writer.
21. **A managed Blender under a package name outside the fixed set has no admissible removal carriage.** Added 2026-08-22 with [E7](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password). [B15](#55-taking-a-blender-off)'s manager-route removal names its package with a constant fixed at build time — the store name [B2](#51-the-list-and-the-fetch) reads and the distribution package Fact 45's apt build carries — because `Server protocol and transfers` 1.1 forbids echoing a machine-read name into a command string in either phase, and none of that rule's four carriages carries a non-path machine-origin identifier into a command — the fourth, machine self-derivation, reaches only the credentials the invoking process itself carries and never a machine-read name. **Still true after 2026-09-23's fifth carriage, and this item is the case that shows where its line is.** That carriage carries a name the *machine's own tool* answers, composed on the machine inside the command; here the name is one **Smoozy read off the machine** at [B10](#54-the-search)'s search and would have to echo back, which is the thing both carriages refuse. **No detection tool on the machine answers *which package holds the Blender the user installed*, so there is nothing to ask and nothing changes.** **Breaks:** a managed build installed under any other name is found by [B10](#54-the-search)'s search and warned about under [B14](#55-taking-a-blender-off), and cannot be removed by Smoozy — the removal is reported as one this module cannot perform, and the user's own manager is the route. Row 96's warning is unaffected. **What holds:** the folder-deletion route entire, whose paths are file-transfer-subsystem protocol fields and need no command; and both constant-name manager routes.
22. **CLOSED 2026-08-22, the same day it was opened, by running it. The route exists and [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 5 and [M1b](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each) now carry it.** On a rented `Debian 12` machine with an L40 and no driver at all: enable `contrib non-free non-free-firmware`, run **`nvidia-detect`** — Debian's own equivalent of Canonical's tool, which named `nvidia-driver` in its own words — install it, **restart**, and `nvidia-smi` answers. **149 seconds plus one restart, nothing asked of a human** ([The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md)). **Two things travel with the closure and are written into the route rather than left here:** the stock image reaches `main` only, so the driver is unreachable until the components are enabled; and **the restart is not optional**, because the headers for the kernel a stale image booted may not be in the archive at all — `linux-headers-6.1.0-31-amd64` was absent and DKMS built against `6.1.0-52-amd64`. **The break below is spent**, and Row 88's promise is kept on this family rather than degrading to a refusal. **What the closure does not settle** is `ubuntu-drivers`' Debian absence being permanent — `apt-cache policy ubuntu-drivers-common` returned nothing at all on this image, which is a reading of one image. ~~The original entry follows.~~ **Debian's driver route is not established, and it is the one step of the sequence that does not carry across the supported pair.** Added 2026-08-22 with the Creator's Ubuntu-and-Debian ruling. [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 5 installs through `ubuntu-drivers install --gpgpu`, which is Canonical's own tool; **nothing this project has read or run establishes an equivalent on Debian**, and no driver install has ever been run there. [M1b](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each) names it rather than borrowing Ubuntu's answer. **Breaks:** on a Debian machine whose card has no working driver, the ready action performs every other step and declines the driver one, so Row 88's promise — the machine made able to render — degrades to a refusal on that machine. **What holds:** the direction, which is the safe one. The check-first order runs `nvidia-smi` before anything, so a Debian machine that already has a working driver — which is what a provider's Debian image would normally ship — passes the step untouched and is fully prepared; and where the check fails, [W4](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s refusal to claim the machine renders on its cards means the user is told the machine is not ready rather than given a wrong render. **What it does not break:** the rest of the sequence, which is Debian's own — the repair operation quotes dpkg's and Debian Policy's manuals, and `tar`'s Essential status is Debian Policy's rather than Ubuntu's. **Answerable, and cheap:** one Debian machine with an NVIDIA card and no driver, which the second provider read on 2026-08-22 offers as a plain image; read what that distribution documents as its own driver route and run it once.
23. **ANSWERED 2026-08-22, and the answer is that it is neither family's property — it is the image's.** Observed on both sides the same day ([The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md)): **Hyperstack's `Debian 12 Plain` carries it** — `unattended-upgrades 2.9.1+nmu3` installed, `/etc/apt/apt.conf.d/20auto-upgrades` setting both periodic keys to `1`, `apt-daily.timer` and `apt-daily-upgrade.timer` both **enabled**, one of them **17 minutes from firing** when read. **Vast's `ubuntu:24.04` container carries none of it** — not installed, no configuration file, no timers, and no service manager to run them. **So the Ubuntu-default framing was the wrong axis**, and [S7b](#43-why-each-step-is-safe-to-run-again)'s probe-rather-than-assume rule is vindicated in both directions rather than in one. **Nothing breaks and nothing is owed** — the rule already probes the lock. ~~The original entry follows.~~ **`unattended-upgrades` running by default is Ubuntu's own behaviour and is not established for Debian.** Added 2026-08-22. [S7b](#43-why-each-step-is-safe-to-run-again)'s busy-before-broken rule rests on Canonical's documentation that the service *"may often run on system startup"*; Debian is not established to ship it enabled. **Breaks:** nothing. The rule probes the lock rather than assuming it is held, so on a machine where the service never runs the bounded wait simply never elapses. Recorded so that no later reading treats the Ubuntu default as a property of the pair.
24. **What an add-on carrying a compiled part does across the shared settings folder at the Python change has never been tried. Added 2026-08-31, out of the repaired Blender research.** All five sharing tests on record used a plain-Python add-on with no bundled module, so the install-time version check was never exercised across the change; [A6](#6-add-ons)'s shared-folder case rests on the folder rule and on the compare-before-install, not on an observation of that shape. **Breaks:** nothing this module does — A6 installs nothing where the listing shows the add-on present, whichever way the untried case would go. What is weaker is what the module may say about *when* a wrong-Python compiled part shows itself on a shared folder: at use, on [A5](#6-add-ons)'s claim discipline, rather than at a loud refusal. **What holds:** A5's judge-by-read-back, and the claim already cut to installed and switched on, loadable at the moment it was switched on. **Answerable, and free:** one machine holding 5.0 and 5.1, an add-on with a compiled part installed into the shared folder under one of them, then asked for under the other.
25. **Two of this project's own records disagree about what a fresh Blender's card list holds, and no observation will settle it. Added 2026-09-02.** [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md) records that from factory startup, on a rented multi-card box in both Blender versions, **every individual device in the list arrives with `use = True`** while the backend selector arrives `NONE`. The repaired Blender research records the same list coming back **empty** in all four versions — untouched settings folder, and again at factory settings — filling only once a refresh is asked for ([R1a](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of)). Both are labelled **observed in a test** and **neither is doubted**. **The batch's own record does not say whether its script called a refresh**, so the two cannot be reconciled from anything written down, and the Creator ruled on 2026-08-31 that there will be **no further Blender research and no further rented machine** — so nothing will settle it by observation. **Breaks:** any clause that would read a device list to decide what a machine has. **What holds, and it is why this stops nothing here:** no clause of this document reads that list — card presence is [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 1's bus scan and driver query, the driver check is item 5's, [W1](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine)'s *Could not be made ready* turns on the driver never answering, and [W4](#10-what-this-module-says-about-a-machine) reads a finished job's recorded device line. **And R1a's rule is correct on either record**: both agree a list read after a refresh returns the machine's devices, so refreshing first costs nothing and is the only reading that is safe under both. `Servers` [Section 16](Servers.md#16-unresolved) item 24 and `Rendering`'s sources file M13 carry the same conflict from their own sides.
26. **CLOSED 2026-09-04, out of a build sitting: both owed things were written. The gate clause is `Rendering` S3a, ahead of that document's S4 unit, with its own residue at that document's §9 item 27; the row is Row 517, in `Rows/Server Setup.md`, quoting the spec sentence and answered at S3a. Nothing in this document changed, which is what the entry said would happen.** The original entry follows, unchanged. **A live spec promise added 2026-09-01 is answered by nothing in this stage, and the clause that answers it is not this document's. Added 2026-09-02 at the stage-close gate repair; routed to the runner.** `Preparation/Specs/Server Setup.md` promises *"Smoozy therefore refuses such a job before it starts rather than sending it, and says why"* for the case where the user picks the engine themselves and the machine's Blender lacks it — the case that is *"not a wrong picture, it is an open bill"*, one of the four Blender versions running until somebody destroys the machine. **[A9a](#6-add-ons) states this document's half**: the refusal belongs to `Rendering`, ahead of its S4 submission unit, and what this module supplies is [Section 12](#12-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)'s standing line — [A5](#6-add-ons)'s read-back of what the target build has installed and switched on, under [B1](#5-the-blender-lifecycle)'s bar that a machine is read by asking the build and never by parsing a list. **Owed, and in files this document may not write:** the gate clause itself in `Rendering`, and **one row quoting the spec sentence**, which `Coverage.md` has for nothing today. **Breaks:** the promise is kept by nobody — a user-picked engine the machine lacks is sent, and on the one version that never stops the user meets a bill rather than a failure. **What holds without it:** A5's read-back exists and is already supplied, so the fact the gate needs is on hand the moment the clause is written; and this document changes not at all whichever way it is written, which is why the routing is the whole of the repair here. **Recorded for the runner; `Rendering` is not edited from here and no row file was opened.**
27. **Whether either driver route leaves the OptiX library on the machine is not established past one image. Added 2026-10-03 with `Rendering` L23's ruling that NVIDIA cards render with OptiX.** Ubuntu: Fact 47 saw `ubuntu-drivers install --gpgpu` leave `libnvoptix` out on one Hyperstack image, and [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 5's fifth act, which puts it on, has not been run. Debian: the metapackage route was read for the driver and the card query only (item 22), never for the library. **Breaks:** a machine Smoozy set up whose cards jobs end at `Rendering` L24b. **What holds:** that ending is before the render instruction, loud, in plain words, and nothing is paid for; the first pass on each family shows which way it goes.
28. **Whether a machine Smoozy can manage is told apart from one it cannot, for the driver, is not established past the kind reading. Added 2026-10-11, the Creator's rulings on OptiX and the driver.** [R1](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of) item 5 brings a driver below the OptiX bar up only on a systemd machine, and tells the user on a container. That rests on `Rented-server runtime` K1's kind and [M2](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each)'s table. **Nothing reads whether the driver can actually be replaced.** Creator-stated, 2026-10-11: RunPod and Vast are containers, Hyperstack is not, and real users will rent from others. **Breaks, where the two part ways:** a machine read as systemd whose driver still cannot be replaced meets the swap, which fails. The cost is a slow failed step, and the user is then told in `Servers` H11b's words. If the failure leaves no driver answering, a machine that rendered older Blenders before setup ends *Could not be made ready*. **Since [R1b](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of), that failure can come from a swap run nobody pressed for** — one that followed an install, or started once the server's work ended — so the machine that stops rendering is one the user only installed a Blender on. *Added 2026-10-11, later the same day, the Creator's ruling that the driver follows every Blender on the server.* **And on Debian**, the family's route installed 535 on Debian 12, below 5.2's bar, and whether it offers a newer branch is not read. So on that family the swap may not reach the bar at all, and the user is told the same way. **What holds:** nothing is forced on a container, a driver that does not answer is never called ready, and every case that misses the bar ends with the user told.
29. **A job held for the swap waits on the user's connection, and on an elevation the user may decline. Added 2026-10-11, later still, the Creator's ruling that a job needing the newer driver is held back ([R1c](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of)).** R1b's swap is started by the desktop while connected, and nothing on the machine starts it. **Breaks:** with Smoozy closed, a held job waits however long that is, while the server's other work runs, and the server may then stand idle with only that job on it. Where elevation is owed and the user declines it, the swap is not asked for again in that connected period, and the job waits for the next one. **Also: a hold blocks a package commit** (`Rented-server runtime` U25, G23), so while a driver hold stands, `Desktop app and delivery` V11's update of Smoozy's program on that server waits too. **Where the swap run needs a newer program than the server holds, the two wait on each other**, and the job stays held until the user cancels it or has the update done anyway (V15's act, which stops and clears the jobs there). Whether the swap run needs a newer program than the one that placed the hold is the builder's to settle; O3b's account names a possible new run kind. *Settled 2026-10-12 at Build, held-job piece A: it does not. The swap run is the ready action's own kind and places no package, and a ready action on a machine this copy set up places none either, so the two never wait on each other; and no hold for the driver is sent to a program older than this application's own ([R1c](#21-what-the-action-is-made-of)'s dated bullet). No auditor has seen this text.* **What holds:** the entry says what the job waits for and that it needs Smoozy connected (`Queue & History` H12); nothing expires the hold; the user can cancel it at any time; the idle statement still fires for a server whose only work is such a job (`Queue & History` W1). *No auditor has seen this text.*
30. **Settled, 2026-11-03, by the 5.3 live passes, on the beta: `ldd ./blender` finds nothing missing on bare images of Vast, RunPod and Sesterce; Vulkan's library is Blender's own, and the driver supplies the rest. What `ldd` over `lib/` misses (`libamdhip64`, `libze_loader`, on two images `libXt` and `libOpenGL`) stopped nothing rendered.** ~~What 5.3 starts on under Linux, and whether a bare image has what it needs. Added 2026-11-02, the 5.3 integration's piece 6; no auditor has seen it.~~ By its release notes 5.3 moves Linux's default drawing system to Vulkan, and what it does where Vulkan is missing is untried (*The machine 1*, *What a render demands 7*). **Holds regardless:** R1 item 4, where a library the mapping does not name fails the step in the machine's own words; `WHAT_BLENDER_OPENS_BY_NAME` holds `libEGL.so.1` alone. **Settles it:** the 5.3 live pass, reading `ldd` on a bare image and rendering; it runs the app, so the ruling at this section's head does not reach it. `Rendering` §9 item 29 is the render's half.
31. **Nothing about 5.3's published files was read: its archive, its checksum file, or what unpacking it needs (*Builds 10*). Added 2026-11-02, the 5.3 integration's piece 6; no auditor has seen it.** **Holds regardless:** B4's derived address, B5's saying so and naming the version, and the checksum and tooling steps. **Settles it:** 5.3.0's release folder read at the release re-read, and an install on a rental.
32. **That two copies of 5.3 share one settings folder is inferred, not tested (*Builds 5*). Added 2026-11-02, the 5.3 integration's piece 6; no auditor has seen it.** 4.5 to 5.2 were tested. **Holds regardless:** with add-ons parked Smoozy writes nothing to that folder, so sharing it costs nothing. It matters again only if add-ons return. **Settles it:** two 5.3 copies on one rental.
33. **Removing a Blender while it renders was not tested for 5.3 (*Builds 8*). Added 2026-11-02, the 5.3 integration's piece 6; no auditor has seen it.** **Holds regardless:** B14's warning names every live job on the build being removed, and a render that dies is reported as failed. **Settles it:** removing a 5.3 during a render on a rental.

---

## 14. Moments that reached the Creator
