**One, and it was ruled on 2026-08-22: which Linux this module prepares.** Added the same day. **The moment:** this document's whole preparation sequence is written in one distribution family's terms — the driver-detection command is Canonical's, the repair sequence and the frontend-lock setting are apt's and dpkg's, the package names are that family's — while [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md) promised the user *"Linux servers and no other kind"* without narrowing further. **That is `Method`'s first escalation exactly**: the architecture could not keep the promise the spec made, and what would replace it — a machine prepared with another family's commands — is a machine left worse than it was found. Narrowing what the user is promised is a Specs change and not this stage's to take.

**What was put to the Creator, and what decided it.** They were shown **a sample of what is rentable** rather than an argument, and it is stated here at the width the project's own record gives it. On one provider, **1,700 offers sampled across several orderings** were Ubuntu and nothing else, 20.04 through 26.04 — the figure's home is [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md)'s platform boundary and it carries its own bound there, *"which is a sample of what it rents and not the whole of it"*; **no method, no query and no address for that sampling is recorded anywhere in `Preparation/`**, so it is a figure the project holds rather than one a reader can re-take. On a second provider, the image list offered **Ubuntu, Debian and AlmaLinux** ([The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md), image lists read 2026-08-22). **Two corrections, both 2026-08-23.** This paragraph read *what is actually rentable*, dropping the caveat the spec that owns the figure states in the same sentence — the caveat is restored. And it gave the second provider as *seventeen images … fifteen Ubuntu, one Debian and one AlmaLinux*; **those counts appear nowhere in `Preparation/` but here and no record of the run that produced them exists, so they are struck** and the recorded families stand in their place. **What is lost by the strike is the proportion** — the second provider's list is known to hold all three families and not in what numbers, so this document no longer says how thin the Debian and AlmaLinux end of it was. So *any Linux* was a promise with almost no machines behind it, on that sample, that Ubuntu does not already cover, while keeping it would have meant a per-family setup for machines the sample did not find on offer. **They ruled: Ubuntu and Debian.** Label: **Creator-stated** (2026-08-22). The spec carries it in *Getting the machine ready* and in [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md)'s platform boundary; both decisions records carry the line saying no auditor has seen it. **What this document gained:** [M1a](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each)'s family reading and its refusal, [M1b](#1-the-machine-kind-and-what-a-setup-can-be-on-each)'s per-family driver route, and [Section 13](#13-unresolved) items 22 and 23, which name what Debian does not inherit from Ubuntu rather than assuming it does.

**The stage's oldest escalation** — Rows 105 and 283 against Fact 51 — **is spent**: the fact is answered yes, on the record of 2026-08-13, and no stop was opened. **Row 114 was worked to a kept promise rather than an escalation**: Fact 50's negative was a stub with no guard, and [Section 4](#4-interruption-resumption-and-the-safe-re-run) keeps the promise on two clauses of `Rented-server runtime` rather than one — **A5b**, which refuses the second submission at the admission point naming the open run, and **§9 P8**, which is what a run that did start does with a state it does not own — the runner's reading, confirmed by the working rather than taken on trust. **A5b named here 2026-08-29 at the stage-close gate**, which found this sentence and the Fact 50 paragraph both giving the whole mechanism to P8; the refusal half was P8's only by attribution, and [S4](#42-the-recovery-run) item 1 now cites the same pair.

**Twelve answers were checked against `Method`'s escalations and declined, and the checks are recorded because no gate catches the third.** The ninth was added 2026-08-22 with `Job files and outputs` L4a's hand-over half; the tenth and eleventh 2026-08-23, with Rows 439 and 440; the twelfth 2026-08-25, with Row 489.

- **Row 88 on a machine that cannot be elevated on, and the Secure Boot machine.** Both close as a machine that may not be set up at all, which the spec says in as many words — a narrowing stated, not a promise broken. Not the first escalation; checked and declined.
- **Row 117's middle lifetime spanning a machine reboot** ([E5](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password)). The user typed a password for the life of the connection, and the connected period surviving a Smoozy-caused restart is the spec's own construction — nothing new to type, no new place to look, nothing that can be lost. Declined.
- **Row 108's wait** ([T6](#8-the-restart)). The user sits before a half-finished setup while the machine returns — the spec names that moment itself, grants the stop, and the give-up lands on the part-finished reporting the spec already describes. Nothing new reaches them. Declined.
- **The recovery refused while a dead group still validates** ([S5](#42-the-recovery-run)), **re-stated on what the user actually meets.** In the ordinary case the group ends at a boundary and the cost is a wait. In the case the defunct-entry hole creates — a dead group reading as live on a machine whose supervisor is up — the cost is larger and is not a wait: the machine admits nothing at all until the state is cleared, and **no restart is offered as a way out**, because [T1](#8-the-restart) issues one only as part of a setup step that needs it, none does here, and a restart written now for this purpose would be a restart no setup step needs, which Row 283's own words exclude. **Still declined**, and on the ground the re-statement leaves standing: Row 114's promise can be kept by a named, existing route — the reap `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 4 already records as owed, carried here as this document's requirement at [Section 13](#13-unresolved) item 16 — so this is a promise that got harder to keep rather than one that cannot be kept, which `Method` closes as unresolved with what breaks written down. The opposite ruling — starting the recovery beside a possibly-live run — is Fact 50's demonstrated harm.
- ~~**The elevated retry offered when the search could not read every folder** ([B11](#54-the-search)).~~ **No longer offered, 2026-10-02, on the Creator's ruling at B11; the entry is kept as it was decided.** The user opened a flow to look at what is installed and meets a password prompt, at each of [B12](#54-the-search)'s three moments. **Declined**: Row 117 grants the offer for **any step of this module refused for lack of permission**, and the spec lists searching the disk for the Blenders already on it among what this module runs on a machine, so the offer is what the spec says the user gets rather than a cost it does not name. **The flow reaches the offer only after a refusal and never before it** ([E2](#9-elevation-the-refusal-and-the-password)'s timing rule, which is the spec's own), and where the user declines, B11's honest shortfall is the whole outcome — the refusals counted, the answer saying the machine could not be searched completely.
- **A setup run ended by the command-gap window** ([Y7](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)). The user meets *cut off part-way*, which the spec grants for a lost connection; the window only makes the machine's side of that truth arrive without waiting forever. Declined.
- **The boundary-resume re-running a step's tail** ([S7](#43-why-each-step-is-safe-to-run-again)). Bytes re-fetched and packages re-checked cost machine time on a paid machine and nothing else; the alternative — trusting unverified partial state — is the corrupt-quietly shape this stage has met five times. Declined.
- **The one-run-at-a-time refusal** ([Y9](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)). A second setup on one machine is refused with the first reported — the spec's own rule made mechanical; several machines still run at once. Declined.
- **The second mount taken over where `Job files and outputs` L4a places the package area apart** ([R6](#23-what-the-placement-is-given), [R7](#23-what-the-placement-is-given)). One further attached volume of the user's is owned by the supervisor's login after an elevated `chown`, and nothing undoes it — which is the cost [R6](#23-what-the-placement-is-given) refuses to pay for a mount that wins no choice. **Declined**: it is the same act R7 already performs on the root, on a mount Smoozy is about to place its own material on; the user is never asked which mount (`Job files and outputs` L5), meets no new place to look, types nothing, and loses nothing; the path is recorded ([R7b](#23-what-the-placement-is-given)) where the refused case would have recorded nothing; and it is bounded to the one or two mounts a choice actually landed on. **On the ordinary machine with one writable candidate it does not happen at all.**
- **The interrupted job left held back by the user's own stop** ([Y8b](#33-the-worker-the-driving-and-the-stop)). The user stops a machine-changing run, the state stands, and the render the setup interrupted is not re-run until a later run reaches *done*. **Declined**: the spec grants the stop and says in the same breath what it leaves — *"Stopping leaves the same situation a lost connection leaves"* and *"Running it again from here is how the machine is put right"* — and [S1](#41-what-a-part-finished-setup-reports) and [S2](#41-what-a-part-finished-setup-reports) put that state in front of the user on the next connection. The job is held, not lost; nothing new is typed and no new place to look appears. It is [T6a](#8-the-restart)'s already-declined case reached by a control instead of by neglect.
- **The Blender table's folder link** ([B12a](#54-the-search)). It can land the user in a folder that is read-only, or gone since the reading. **Declined**: the link is Design's own promise, the landing is `File Browser` P3's ordinary case, and what the user meets there is that module's honest answer rather than anything this document composed. Withholding the link on some rows would have been the new cost, not offering it.
- **The declined below-floor install's discard** ([B7a](#52-a-build-the-user-points-at)). The user declines and the staged, unpacked copy on the machine is gone; changing their mind costs the upload again, which at an observed 0.62 to 1.3 MB/s up, priced at the slower on `Server protocol and transfers` S12 step 3's settled rule of 2026-08-23, is real time. (**Corrected 2026-08-29**: this read *at an observed 1.2 MB/s up*, cited to Fact 54, which carries no uplink figure, and priced a user-facing cost at the faster of the two readings of that one line against the settled rule in the document that owns the transfer contract. **The decline is unmoved** — it never rested on the rate — and the cost it names is about twice as large, which is the direction that makes the check harder rather than easier.) **Declined**: the way out is the spec's own grant, the file on the user's computer is untouched so nothing of theirs is lost, and the alternative — a declined build kept in staging indefinitely — is an invisible store on a paid machine that the sweep contract forbids and no spec grants a place to look at.

---

## 15. What this document does not own

Cited, and deliberately not restated or decided here.

- **The setup-in-progress state's mechanism, writer and lifetime; the package transaction entire; the supervisor, the workers, the admission point, the stop sequence and the execution-group validation; the captured streams; health readings** — `Rented-server runtime`, Rows 286 and its eighteen sections. Rows 91 and 110 keep their homes here and cite its §5.3 and §9.
- **The pre-package surface, the bootstrap-and-repair request's shape and idempotency, the transfer contract, capability negotiation, per-waiter deadlines, and the free-space reading** — `Server protocol and transfers`, Rows 281, 287, 289, 291, 306.
- **The root's choice and the package area's own location apart from it, the five areas, every job path, the manifest, the launch material, the redirect and the completeness readings** — `Job files and outputs`, Rows 118 to 139 and 297. This document supplies the candidate set and performs both hand-overs ([R6, R7](#23-what-the-placement-is-given)); neither choice is made here.
- **The launch contract, the version confirmation's conduct, the device line's ask and route, stop, re-run and carry-on** — `Rendering`, Rows 1 to 23 and 294. Nothing there installs, removes or elevates.
- **The live set, what becomes of a waiting job whose Blender was removed, and the re-run after a machine went down** — `Queue & History`, Rows 47 to 87 and 302.
- **The connection, the connected period, reconnect policy and attempts, host trust, the server label, hardware readings as a user-facing promise** — `Servers`, Rows 24 to 46, unwritten; this document's T4 and T6 are written so its half is a citation.
- **The version match, the automatic pick, the mismatch warning, Pre-load's reading and the submission payload's composition** — `Render Settings`, Rows 140 to 158, unwritten; [Section 12](#12-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document) states what it takes from here.
- **The secret store, the token's placement rules, logging redaction, and the erasure order** — `Security and licence`, Row 278.
- **The bot, the slot, the board, the told-record and the role worker's conduct** — `Telegram`, Rows 255 to 277 and 296; §20's material list is placed here and decided there.
- **The automatic supervisor-unavailable repair trigger and every presentation** — `Desktop app and delivery`, Rows 280, 282, 284, 288, 290, 303, 304 and 305.
- **Identities, desired against observed state, reconciliation on both sides, the event journal, retention** — `State, identity and recovery`, Rows 285 and 292.
